title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/france">France</a>'s UN ambassador Jerome Bonnafont, president of the Security Council for September, told reporters without disclosing the outcome.</p><p>According to diplomats, Rebeca Grynspan led the third round with nine “encourage” votes and five “discourage” votes. Carolyn Rodrigues-Birkett of Guyana followed with eight “encourage” and six “discourage” votes, while Argentina’s <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/europe/2026/05/29/rafael-grossi-hits-out-at-absent-un-in-global-wars/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/europe/2026/05/29/rafael-grossi-hits-out-at-absent-un-in-global-wars/">Rafael Grossi</a> received five “encourage” and eight “discourage” votes.</p><p>"The race now has two clear frontrunners: Grynspan and Rodrigues-Birkett. But neither has locked down enough Council support to escape the spectre of a possible P5 veto," Daniel Forti, head of UN affairs at the International Crisis Group told <i>The National.</i></p><p>"The next vote, likely immediately after High-Level Week, will show whether they have staying power - or whether the race is likely to open up to new candidates."</p><p>No clear frontrunner emerged from the first two rounds, with eight candidates now seeking to succeed Secretary General <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/07/25/guterres-arrives-in-syria-for-first-visit-by-a-un-chief-in-17-years/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/07/25/guterres-arrives-in-syria-for-first-visit-by-a-un-chief-in-17-years/">Antonio Guterres</a> when his term ends in December.</p><p>Several council members have said they would like to see a woman lead the UN for the first time in its 81-year history.</p><p>Former Guyanese foreign minister Ms Rodrigues-Birkett narrowly led the second straw poll, followed by Costa Rica's Ms Grynspan and Mr Grossi, director general of the International Atomic Energy Agency.</p><p>The other candidates are former Senegalese president Macky Sall, Ecuador's Ivonne Baki, former Chilean president Michelle Bachelet, former Ecuadorian foreign minister Maria Fernanda Espinosa and Uganda's Olara Otunnu.</p><p>Diplomats have also raised the possibility that other candidates could enter the race as the five veto-wielding permanent members of the Security Council - Britain, China, France, Russia and the US - seek consensus.</p><p>A candidate needs at least nine votes in the 15-member Security Council and must avoid a veto from any of the five permanent members. The council then recommends a candidate to the 193-member General Assembly for appointment.</p><p>The US has not publicly backed a candidate. A State Department official said on Thursday that contenders should commit to getting the UN "back to basics and publicly commit to the changes we require."</p><p>"The United States is looking for a practical, driven, and reform-focused candidate who will safeguard the valuable contributions of Member States, restore competent management and streamline the organisation significantly," the official said.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content National]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 15:28:26 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/01/19/president-sheikh-mohamed-arrives-in-india-to-begin-working-visit/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/01/19/president-sheikh-mohamed-arrives-in-india-to-begin-working-visit/">President Sheikh Mohamed</a> and Indian Prime Minister <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/05/12/indian-prime-minister-narendra-modi-to-make-official-visit-to-the-uae-on-friday/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/05/12/indian-prime-minister-narendra-modi-to-make-official-visit-to-the-uae-on-friday/">Narendra Modi</a> held a phone call on Friday to explore ways to further strengthen deep-rooted ties between their nations.</p><p>The two leaders explored efforts to bolster bilateral relations - which span more than 50 years - under the Comprehensive Strategic Partnership and the Comprehensive Economic Partnership Agreement between the UAE and India.</p><p>Sheikh Mohamed also congratulated on his recent birthday and wished him continued good health and further success in driving India's progress and fulfilling the aspirations of its people, state news agency Wam reported.</p><p>Mr Modi, who has served as prime minister for more than 12 years, turned 76 on Thursday.</p><p>He thanked Sheikh Mohamed for his sentiments and set out his intention to continue to work closely with the UAE President for the benefit of both countries.</p><p>The two sides also discussed a number of issues of mutual interest, with a focus on efforts to boost regional security and stability.</p><h2><b>Flourishing friendship</b></h2><img " height="3775" width="5000"/><p>India and the <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/uae/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/uae/">UAE</a> enjoy a decades-long alliance that dates back to the formation of the Emirates. The UAE is now home to about four million Indian citizens, the largest expatriate community in the country.</p><p>The UAE signed a Comprehensive <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/economy/2025/02/05/uae-foreign-trade-hits-record-dh3-trillion-in-2024-as-cepas-drive-growth/" target="_blank" rel="">Economic</a> Partnership Agreement with India in 2022 – the first Cepa signed by the Emirates – pushing bilateral trade between the countries beyond $85 billion. The agreement made India the UAE's largest trading partner, with the UAE becoming India's third-largest trading partner.</p><p>Sheikh Mohamed and Mr Modi regularly speak by phone and have met in both India and the UAE in recent years to further robust links.</p><p>In May, Mr Modi made an official visit to the UAE, during which he held high-level talks with Sheikh Mohamed.</p><p><a href="https://www.mohamedbinzayed.ae/en/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.mohamedbinzayed.ae/en/">Sheikh Mohamed</a> had paid a working visit to New Delhi in January, where he and Mr Modi pledged to deepen co-operation in several sectors – from technology and artificial intelligence, to the economy and food security – during discussions at the Prime Minister's residence in the Indian capital.</p><p>Mr Modi previously made a <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/uae/2024/02/14/alhan-modi-uae-abu-dhabi/" target="_blank" rel="">two-day visit to the UAE</a> in 2024, where he addressed a crowd of <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/uae/2024/02/13/live-modi-uae-india-abu-dhabi/" target="_blank" rel="">40,000 Indians at Zayed Sports City Stadium</a>. He also spoke at the <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/opinion/editorial/2024/02/12/world-governments-summit-has-a-role-in-shaping-the-world/" target="_blank" rel="">World Governments Summit</a> in Dubai, before inaugurating the first <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/uae/heritage/2024/02/13/uae-and-india-unite-in-creation-of-abu-dhabis-first-hindu-temple/" target="_blank" rel="">Hindu temple in Abu Dhabi</a>.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/7DPOKHWAOJHGFBRB4EYWQCHMYU.jpg?auth=93cb8cbd8dd1348eb4e4b5fc23caafe4910426d95b6972bdf9afd6d695b34324&amp;smart=true&amp;width=4800&amp;height=2700" type="image/jpeg" height="2700" width="4800"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[President Sheikh Mohamed held a phone call with Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi on Friday.  Crown Prince Court / Reuters]]></media:description></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Netflix's Physical 100: Italy eliminations – Who's out, who's still in and what are the challenges?]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2026/09/18/netflix-physical-100-italy-eliminations-ranking/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2026/09/18/netflix-physical-100-italy-eliminations-ranking/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[David Tusing]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 14:25:47 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>From Olympic champions to rugby players, fighters and celebrities, 100 contestants entered the arena in <i>Physical 100: Italy</i>, hoping to prove they have the ultimate physique and stamina to face gruelling fitness challenges.</p><p>The Italian spin-off of Netflix's hit South Korean reality competition <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2025/11/03/physical-100-neflix-versions-usa-italy-asia/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2025/11/03/physical-100-neflix-versions-usa-italy-asia/"><i>Physical: 100</i></a> had its premiere on September 11, with the first three episodes immediately cutting the field from 100 contestants to 40.</p><p>Among those taking part are swimming great Federica Pellegrini, Olympic diver Tania Cagnotto, former international <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/rugby">rugby</a> player Mirco Bergamasco, Olympic gymnastics champion Jury Chechi, kickboxer and television personality Elisabetta Canalis, former rugby player Alvise Rigo and content creator Luis Sal.</p><p>But, as with the original series, sporting pedigree and celebrity offer no protection once the challenges begin.</p><p>Here is who's still competing, who's been eliminated and how the challenges have unfolded. </p><h2><b>What is the format of Physical 100?</b></h2><p>In the show, 100 men and women known for their physical prowess compete in a series of challenges testing strength, endurance, speed, agility, strategy and mental resilience. There are no separate men's and women's categories, meaning contestants can compete directly against each other regardless of gender.</p><p>For <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2026/08/18/netflix-physical-100-italy-cast-meet-the-olympic-champions-and-celebrities-competing/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2026/08/18/netflix-physical-100-italy-cast-meet-the-olympic-champions-and-celebrities-competing/"><i>Physical 100: Italy</i></a>, locally titled <i>Physical Italia: da 100 a 1</i>, the contestants range in age from 22 to 60, and come from a variety of sporting backgrounds. Netflix said more than 1,000 potential contestants were interviewed before the final 100 were selected. </p><p>The line-up includes Olympians, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/mma">MMA</a> fighters, bodybuilders, CrossFit and <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/wellbeing/2025/03/05/what-is-hyrox-fitness-competition-uae/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/wellbeing/2025/03/05/what-is-hyrox-fitness-competition-uae/">Hyrox athletes</a>, as well as competitors from swimming, rugby, gymnastics, kickboxing, triathlon, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/wrestling">wrestling</a> and American football.</p><p>Contestants are eliminated as the challenges progress until only one is crowned the winner. </p><h2><b>What's at stake?</b></h2><p>The 100 contestants are competing for a €100,000 ($118,000) prize. Winners of the original South Korean series, which has completed two seasons, received 300 million won ($210,000).</p><h2><b>What happens in the first challenge?</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/KXFIOTDYYJBEVEFMOCTYLCOWZI.png?auth=ffdcf768295ab437e12c06c2604bf9c1e6897b8f7a725ff846e9f1f8fc21181f&smart=true&width=1920&height=1080" alt="Contestants are suspended above a swimming pool and must support themselves for as long as possible, in a test of grip and stamina. Photo: Netflix" height="1080" width="1920"/><p>The competition begins with all 100 contestants entering the Hall of Busts, where each is represented by a sculpture of their torso.</p><p>The first challenge tests one of the central ideas behind <i>Physical 100</i>: different body types can have very different advantages depending on the task.</p><p>In the endurance test, contestants are suspended above a swimming pool and must support themselves on a structure for as long as possible, testing their grip and upper-body strength as well as their stamina.</p><p>It is a challenge seemingly tailor-made for former gymnast Chechi. Nicknamed the Lord of the Rings after dominating the still rings discipline during his career, he sails through the test on <i>Physical 100: Italy</i>. Breakdancer Mattia Schinco also makes it look easy, even doing push-ups as others struggle to hold on.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/4P7FHA6MXNHWBB6XLLA5DOUXCQ.png?auth=18c532caef51a0f0fa27c55fb8b8b21bdad1b8e274f461df365a145338a5d420&smart=true&width=1920&height=1080" alt="Kickboxer and television personality Elisabetta Canalis in the first challenge. Photo: Netflix" height="1080" width="1920"/><p>But it is CrossFit athlete Elisa Fuliano who emerges victorious, outlasting the entire field by staying on the bar for more than one hour and 27 minutes.</p><p>At the other end of the leader board, the challenge proves particularly difficult for 156kg strongman Andrea Invernizzi, powerlifter Mauro Oldani, bodybuilder Gabriele Di Via and skeleton racer Valentina Margaglio, who are among those to fall into the pool early on.</p><p>Unlike similar preliminary challenges in previous editions of Physical 100, this one carries immediate consequences. The bottom 20 contestants are eliminated, leaving 80 to continue in the competition. </p><h2><b>What is the one-on-one challenge?</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/JGSUFZX74ZGG7JQ5G5VUE6KPHI.png?auth=7707a9bd185fe30b0c081c87ff8228b5228f19c1f3716f09db4a690a60954742&smart=true&width=1920&height=1080" alt="Content creator Luis Sal, right, in a one-on-one duel with powerlifter Filippo Pici. Photo: Netflix" height="1080" width="1920"/><p>The second major test will be familiar to fans of <i>Physical: 100</i>. The remaining 80 contestants are divided into two groups, with those in the top 40 given the chance to choose their opponents for a one-on-one duel in one of two arenas. One tests speed and agility, while the other focuses on strength and endurance.</p><p>The rules are simple: the contestants have three minutes to fight for possession of a ball. Whoever has it when the timer runs out wins, while the loser is eliminated.</p><p>The duels produce some surprising results. Olympic champion Chechi, for example, makes a costly choice when he picks MMA fighter Ivano Salerno for a showdown in the agility arena. Salerno proves nimble on his feet and eventually wears down Chechi, bringing one of the show's most decorated sporting careers to an unexpectedly early end.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/OYCCG2LG3JHEPAM5Y4CFYXWFYQ.png?auth=4cb65e07c79c2ddf1682a1db9beb69d60f5aef921e43493e223cfb92b9922223&smart=true&width=1920&height=1080" alt="Jury Chechi, right, faces-off MMA fighter Ivano Salerno in a one-on-one duel. Photo: Netflix" height="1080" width="1920"/><p>In a controversial move, gymnast Andrea Cingolani chooses bodybuilder Beatrice Beni as his opponent. The physically smaller Beni proves no match for Cingolani, whose victory earns him boos and “villain” status from the other contestants.</p><p>Content creator Sal and TV personality Canalis suffer a similar fate after being chosen by considerably bigger opponents. Sal proves no match for powerlifter Filippo Pici, while Canalis loses to CrossFit champion Fuliano in another lopsided duel.</p><p>By the end of the one-on-one contests, 60 of the original 100 competitors are eliminated, leaving just 40 still in the competition.</p><h2><b>What is the Forgotten Temple challenge?</b></h2><p>The competition then moves from individual battles to its first major team challenge.</p><p>The contestants are divided into eight teams of five for the Forgotten Temple. Each team must build a bridge between two elevated platforms before carrying bags of sand across it. After 12 minutes, the team that has transported the greatest amount of sand wins, while the losing team is eliminated.</p><p>The first contest pits a team led by powerlifter Pici against one led by MMA fighter Daniele Scatizzi. Pici's team loses, sending all five members out of the competition.</p><p>Calcio Storico Fiorentino champion Sebastian Rodwell's team then takes on a team led by CrossFit star Fuliano. Despite having won the opening endurance challenge, Fuliano is unable to lead her team to victory and all five are eliminated.</p><p>CrossFit athlete Emanuele Biviano's team is the next to fall, losing to the team led by butcher and bodybuilder Paolo Masella.</p><p>The final contest sees actor and former rugby player Rigo's team take on one led by Olympic wrestling champion Andrea Minguzzi. Rigo's team loses, apparently bringing his run in the competition to an end.</p><p>However, the four losing teams are soon given one final chance to return.</p><h2><b>What is the Pillars of Hercules challenge?</b></h2><p>The eliminated teams face a redemption challenge that will be familiar to fans of <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2025/11/01/physical-asia-winner-netflix-country/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2025/11/01/physical-asia-winner-netflix-country/"><i>Physical: Asia</i></a>: the Pillars of Hercules.</p><p>Each of the four teams must nominate one contestant to represent them, meaning the fate of all five members rests on a single athlete.</p><p>The four chosen contestants must each hold on to two pillars, one in each hand. As the challenge progresses, the pillars gradually fill with water, becoming increasingly heavy. The last contestant to maintain their grip wins and restores their team to the competition.</p><p>Fuliano is the first to let go, ending her team's hopes of returning. Paolo Innocenti is next to fall, followed by Pici.</p><p>That leaves Rigo as the last contestant standing. His victory brings himself and his four teammates back into the competition and leaves 25 contestants remaining.</p><p>But there is another twist.</p><p>Rigo's victory comes with a catch. He is immediately told that he must eliminate one member of the team he has just saved. He chooses windsurfing champion Marco Begalli, with whom he had clashed during the team challenges, reducing his team to four.</p><p>The leaders of the four teams that survived the Forgotten Temple are then given the same brutal task: each must eliminate one of their own five members.</p><p>It adds a new strategic dimension to <i>Physical 100: Italy</i>. Strength and athletic ability are no longer the only considerations, with team leaders also having to decide which contestants they want beside them – and, potentially, which rivals they would rather not face later in the competition.</p><p>Judo champion Emanuele Bruno, former Mr Universe Tommaso Fragassi, stuntman and influencer Pietro Checchi and personal trainer Sara Del Prete are the four contestants sacrificed by their respective team leaders.</p><p>But the show introduces even more twists.</p><p>Rather than being sent home, the four rejected contestants are brought together to form a fifth team, with Bruno chosen as their leader.</p><p>That leaves 20 contestants officially remaining in the competition. But the formation of a new team made up of four athletes rejected by their own teammates sets up an intriguing dynamic for the next stage – and the prospect of some sweet revenge.</p><h2><b>When are new episodes of Physical 100: Italy released?</b></h2><p><i>Physical 100: Italy</i> consists of eight episodes released across four Fridays.</p><p>Episodes one to three were released on September 11. Episodes four and five arrived on September 18, with episodes six and seven coming on September 25.</p><p>The eighth and final episode will be released on October 2, when the winner of <i>Physical 100: Italy</i> will be revealed.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/G5C4TWZAPFGN7I6U7TGERVEIL4.png?auth=5657ee1d81505ba15760e6bbd8581fa186c318ad010dc2163b4a4d29adfbd2f4&amp;smart=true&amp;width=1920&amp;height=1080" type="image/png" height="1080" width="1920"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Actor and rugby player Alvise Rigo in Physical 100: Italy. Photo: Netflix]]></media:description></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Erdogan to compete in 2028 elections, Turkish officials say]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/18/erdogan-to-compete-in-2028-elections-turkish-officials-say/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/18/erdogan-to-compete-in-2028-elections-turkish-officials-say/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Lizzie Porter]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 13:51:52 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/turkey/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/turkey/">Turkey</a> will hold presidential elections in 2028, senior government officials have said, opening the way for incumbent <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/recep-tayyip-erdogan/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/recep-tayyip-erdogan/">Recep Tayyip Erdogan</a> to extend his rule, which could run to three decades. </p><p>“In 2028, an election will be held to re-elect our esteemed President,” Justice Minister Akin Gurlek told legislators from the ruling Justice and Development Party (AKP) in the Turkish city of Adana. </p><p>It follows comments by Mr Erdogan’s chief adviser stating an early election would take place in April 2028 and the President would run, in the first official confirmation that the 72 year old would seek re-election. “If 360 lawmakers back a decision to call a new election, it will be held on April 16, 2028,” Mehmet Ucum was quoted as saying in Turkish media. </p><p>It would be the last election in which Mr Erdogan could run, Mr Ucum said, as reported by pro-government <i>Daily Sabah</i> newspaper. </p><p>Turkey’s presidential election takes place every five years and though the next was scheduled for May 2028, it is now set to take place a month early. </p><p>Turkey’s constitution allows an incumbent president to run for office for two terms but permits a leader to attempt another mandate if parliament calls an early election during their last term. </p><p>“It seems perverse but a president is deemed to have not met the two-term limit if a term was not fully served,” economist and analyst Timothy Ash wrote in a recent briefing note on Turkey’s elections. </p><p>The comments are a departure from previous remarks made by Mr Erdogan, who last year said he would not seek re-election.</p><p>Mr Erdogan has ruled Turkey since 2003, first as Prime Minister and then as President. He has won two elections since a change to a presidential system following a referendum in 2017, which granted additional presidential power. If he won an election in April 2028, it would grant him five more years in office, until 2033, meaning he would have led Turkey for three decades. </p><p>An early election requires backing from three fifths of parliament – 360 MPs – in a vote, which Mr Erdogan’s AKP and its allies do not currently have. They control 326 seats out of 600 in Turkey’s national assembly, meaning they need 34 more votes to trigger the early election.</p><p>However, some observers believe other MPs, including a faction in a key opposition party, the CHP, and the pro-Kurdish DEM Party, could back an early election. </p><p>Although DEM has long been critical of Mr Erdogan’s government, it has been instrumental in a peace process with the militant Kurdistan Workers’ Party (PKK), in which it has called for greater social and political freedom for Turkey’s Kurdish minority, alongside the PKK’s dissolution. </p><p>The CHP splintered this year following a court ruling ousting its leader, Ozgur Ozel, who then set up an alternative opposition party, known as the New Party. The ruling reinstated as CHP leader its former chief Kemal Kilicdaroglu, who had previously lost elections to Mr Erdogan. </p><p>Mr Ozel, whose New Party has attracted enough former Republican People's Party (CHP) legislators to become Turkey’s current main opposition party, has consistently called for an early election. An opinion poll last month by the Ankara-based Metropoll showed AKP behind New Party, and Mr Ozel has previously said an early election would give voters the opportunity to decide on their leader as a years-long economic crisis drags on. The war in Iran and high energy prices have contributed to a slower decrease in double-digit inflation than the Turkish government had hoped, and household incomes continue to be squeezed by price rises. </p><p>Mr Ozel reacted to presidential adviser Mr Ucum’s remarks on Friday, criticising an early election that would allow Mr Erdogan to stand again weeks before the scheduled date. </p><p>“They can’t escape this election, this ballot, by trying to fool the public with something like, ‘We held it one month early, so this is an early election.’ We will get to that ballot box, and we will win that election,” Mr Ozel said in the Turkish city of Bursa. </p><p>Government critics say there is increasing authoritarianism in Turkey, including frequent arrests of activists, journalists and opposition politicians. Popular Istanbul major Ekrem Imamoglu, who was widely seen as Mr Erdogan’s biggest challenger in any presidential race, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2025/03/24/ekrem-imamoglu-istanbul-mayor-arrest/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2025/03/24/ekrem-imamoglu-istanbul-mayor-arrest/">has been in jail since March 2025</a> on corruption charges. </p><p>Last week a judge handed him a two-year jail sentence and a political ban in one of the numerous cases against him, while trials for others continue. The political ban means even after his release, he would struggle to compete in any future elections. </p><p>At the same time, Mr Erdogan still enjoys widespread public support: he has navigated Turkey through the regional war involving neighbouring Iran, the US and Israel. He has also concretised Ankara’s position <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/07/07/analysis-what-turkey-wants-as-nato-summit-host/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/07/07/analysis-what-turkey-wants-as-nato-summit-host/">as an indispensable partner</a> for the West and the Nato alliance, as well as eastern and African nations. </p><p>Mr Erdogan could also run for a third term by changing the constitution, although observers believe this is a riskier option. That is because it requires either a two-thirds majority in a parliamentary vote, which the AKP and its allies may struggle to obtain, or a three-fifths majority and then a referendum, where the Turkish public could reject the changes. </p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/ROQOGFK7KMBP667EX7TE3C7JFE.jpg?auth=cfe9f9d16f362803c5cabba7c9326c52d645dc8e95793f96bf62dd5d8f129594&amp;smart=true&amp;width=7026&amp;height=4684" type="image/jpeg" height="4684" width="7026"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Turkey's President Recep Tayyip Erdogan Erdogan still enjoys widespread public support. AFP]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">LUIS ROBAYO</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Trump's new tariff threat risks cutting off Asia’s Russian oil lifeline as Gulf supply falls ]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/18/india-china-us-tariffs-russian-oil-lindsey-graham-act/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/18/india-china-us-tariffs-russian-oil-lindsey-graham-act/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Jennifer Gnana, Isaac Arroyo]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 13:20:09 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>The threat of US tariffs on major buyers of Russian oil could squeeze an increasingly important source of crude for India and China as Gulf supply disruption tightens the global market.</p><p>The <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/us/2026/08/07/russia-iran-sanctions-bill-senate/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/us/2026/08/07/russia-iran-sanctions-bill-senate/">US House of Representatives</a> has granted President Donald Trump authority to impose tariffs on any of the five largest buyers of Russian crude or gas that “knowingly” book a new cargo, with rates of up to 100 per cent. The House passed the Lindsey O Graham Sanctioning Russia and Iran Act 262-159 on Wednesday, and a White House official said Mr Trump intends to sign it. </p><p>The tariffs could take effect 30 days after enactment.</p><p>The threat itself could prompt buyers to move faster. “Paradoxically, it could incentivise more purchases from Russia to get ahead of any future risks,” said Rachel Ziemba of Ziemba Insights. “Risks front-loading, buying more now.”</p><p>That could further entrench Russia’s role as a key alternative to Gulf supply for <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2025/11/26/indias-middle-east-crude-pivot-to-reverse-next-year-as-discounts-win/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2025/11/26/indias-middle-east-crude-pivot-to-reverse-next-year-as-discounts-win/">Asian buyers</a>. “Russian crude has effectively become an important supply line and an important hedge against Middle Eastern supply disruption,” said Sumit Ritolia, lead analyst at Kpler. “Replacing Russian crude at current volumes would be challenging or impossible.”</p><blockquote><p>If you keep buying cheap Russian oil to allow this war to continue, we're going to tear the hell out of you, and we're going to crush your economy, because what you're doing is blood money</p><p class="citation">Lindsey Graham, late Republican Senator from South Carolina</p></blockquote><h2><b>'Blood money'</b></h2><p>Mr Graham, who died in July, spent more than a year arguing for that leverage in increasingly forceful terms. “If you keep buying cheap Russian oil to allow this war to continue, we're going to tear the hell out of you, and we're going to crush your economy, because what you're doing is blood money,” he said in a Fox News interview in July 2025, naming China, India and Brazil.</p><p>As Mr Trump’s additional 25 per cent duty on Indian goods took effect that August, Mr Graham wrote: “India is experiencing the cost of supporting [Russian President Vladimir] Putin.” His original bill carried a 500 per cent rate and remained stalled for 15 months.</p><h2><b>Impact on India</b></h2><p>Kpler data shows Russia's total exports were 6.79 million barrels per day in August, down from a peak of 8 million bpd in May. Russian crude accounted for 18.7 per cent of India’s imports in February, on par with Saudi Arabia and Iraq. By July, its share had reached nearly 50 per cent, or 2.96 million bpd, as India’s Middle East imports fell 63 per cent to 1.26 million bpd.</p><p>The US bought $58.9 billion of Indian goods in the first seven months of this year and sold India $30.4 billion worth, US Census Bureau data shows, leaving a $28.4 billion deficit. That was less than a third of the $91.2 billion gap with China over the same period.</p><p>A tariff aimed at Russian oil could also hit Indian exports with little connection to the trade it is trying to punish. Electrical machinery, including smartphones and electronic components assembled for <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/future/technology/2025/05/02/apple-moves-iphone-production-to-india-amid-us-china-trade-war/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/future/technology/2025/05/02/apple-moves-iphone-production-to-india-amid-us-china-trade-war/">American brands such as Apple</a>, was India’s largest export category to the US in the first half of 2026.</p><p>China’s Russian crude imports have fallen 15 per cent since February to 1.89 million bpd, but their share of the country’s imports has risen to 22 per cent from 16 per cent as Gulf supplies declined. China also receives about 800,000 bpd of Russian crude by pipeline, under long-term contracts that would not be affected by tariffs on seaborne shipments.</p><p>The broader oil market is already tight, with <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/18/oil-on-track-to-remain-above-100-for-second-straight-week-as-middle-east-tensions-simmer/">Brent above $100</a> for seven straight sessions. The 10-day average for traffic through the Strait of Hormuz was 14 crossings on Friday, compared with nearly 100 before the war, while <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/14/oil-nears-108-after-saudi-arabia-shuts-pipeline-amid-attacks/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/14/oil-nears-108-after-saudi-arabia-shuts-pipeline-amid-attacks/">Saudi Arabia’s East West Pipeline was damaged</a> in a drone strike last week and its alternative route through Bab Al Mandeb strait remains restricted by Houthi attacks.</p><p>New Delhi’s response on September 17 was firmer than its answers to Mr Graham’s earlier threats. The Ministry of External Affairs said India “remains firmly committed to ensuring energy security for its 1.4 billion people” and would take “all necessary measures to protect its trade and economic interests”.</p><p>Washington’s position has been more ambiguous. At the start of the Iran war, the administration <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/04/19/indias-short-lived-return-to-iranian-oil-ends-as-us-waiver-expires/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/04/19/indias-short-lived-return-to-iranian-oil-ends-as-us-waiver-expires/">briefly allowed India</a> to lift Iranian crude for the first time in seven years to help keep oil flowing and prices down.</p><p>“The White House is likely to be cautious about imposing tariffs in the near term because doing so would tighten supplies further, push oil prices higher and place additional pressure on the global economy,” said Neil Quilliam, associate fellow at Chatham House.</p><p>“The high price at the pump in the US has already hit Trump’s popularity rating.”</p><h2><b>China trade-off</b></h2><p>China could also escape immediate tariffs. Bloomberg reported this week that planned duties will be held back until Mr Trump meets President Xi Jinping. Even tariffs of 20 per cent to 30 per cent “would end the truce, risk critical mineral shortages and additional export controls”, Ms Ziemba said.</p><p>Refiners are likely to seek ways around the measure rather than simply stop buying Russian crude. “China and India are the key pressure points as refiners will seek exemptions, reduce spot purchases and demand wider Russian discounts rather than stop buying altogether,” said Janiv Shah, vice president for oil at Rystad Energy.</p><p>The US tariffs could be “mildly bullish for oil prices as available volume theoretically tightens”.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/EP3NUJFSKCMA3AQM6OUZ5QLBMI.jpg?auth=01159aaf74622f56d27850a7df1541d0171af5570fa7b17d793de0c7fef806f8&amp;smart=true&amp;width=5490&amp;height=3201" type="image/jpeg" height="3201" width="5490"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Lindsay Graham, who died in July, called Indian purchases of Russian oil 'blood money' and threatened the country with 500 per cent trade tariffs. The album is a love letter to her homeland through its musical heritage and geography, and she performed several of its songs in Damascus.</p><p>Assala announced in February that she was working on a project that brings together traditional Syrian folk songs for a new generation.</p><p>“This is the first time in my life that I’ve brought this number of Syrian songs together on one album,” she told <i>ET bel Arabi</i>. “We didn’t play around with them too much, just made some changes in the musical arrangements so that the new generation could connect with them.”</p><p>Here is what to know about the album.</p><h2><b>The songs and regions on record</b></h2><iframe width="560" height="315" src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/cFKcNRSPkLA?feature=oembed" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin" allowfullscreen title="Assala - Om El Aaraeys | Official Music Video 2026 | أصالة - أم العريس"></iframe><p>The album is, in a way, a sonic map of Syria, with each song dedicated to or inspired by the musical traditions of one of the country’s 14 official governorates.</p><p>Damascus is represented by <i>Tawalo</i>, a particularly personal inclusion because it was formerly sung by Assala's father, Mustafa Nasri. Its accompanying video is steeped in nostalgia, evoking the singer's early life.</p><p>Aleppo is represented by <i>Aal Rozana</i>, one of the most recognisable songs in Syrian and wider Levantine repertoire. It has been performed by generations of singers, including Sabah Fakhri, and has become closely associated with Aleppo’s tradition of tarab.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/B25HTQPI6MLD2OOTMUA6TAPZIE.jpg?auth=c3c167a9ff9fe7004c2f2617464590eace610eeaabc43ea34e58cf41e5dea62c&smart=true&width=9000&height=6000" alt="Syrian singer Assala Nasri in Damascus on September 17, her first performance there in more than 16 years. The Daraa outfit reworks elements of traditional dress into a corset and narrow contemporary skirt, while the Hasakah look for <i>Helwa W Taltk Dalal</i> uses embroidery and gold thread.</p><p>For the Damascus anthem, <i>Tawalo</i>, Assala wears a dress inspired by the city’s renowned mosaic woodwork. </p><p>“We completely broke with convention in the Damascus look. The design of the dress evokes the renowned Damascus mosaic woodwork,” stylist Lucy Moussan told <i>Annahar</i>. “The bodice stood out, entirely encrusted with delicate geometric pieces crafted from lustrous natural mother-of-pearl, interspersed with intricate, dark embroidery that closely resembles the luxurious hand-carving on walnut wood.”</p><h2><b>Will there be another Syrian album?</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/LCI2WZSHZMIY2FHXRE7GD3MDHE.jpg?auth=c36e24f0652492419b58312023d06954c51dd5cc7b96d7623fe0ead4dca3f5b0&smart=true&width=9000&height=6000" alt="Assala’s Damascus concert comes less than two weeks after the release of The Syrian Album, featuring one song for each of Syria’s 14 governorates. AFP" height="6000" width="9000"/><p>Assala has already suggested that <i>The Syrian Album</i> may be a one-off, owing to the level of detail required across the songs, arrangements and accompanying videos.</p><p>“I’m determined to pay attention to all the details because this could be my first and last Syrian album,” she told <i>Billboard Arabia</i>. “This is the first time in my life that I’ve brought this number of Syrian songs together on one album.”</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/5DRAZDRKAYCEDYNZ6VV4FSZVAU.jpg?auth=292da5cd03af310bb04ef7074044c40c8a1e9077a45d2a15904289a202ed294a&amp;smart=true&amp;width=9000&amp;height=6000" type="image/jpeg" height="6000" width="9000"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Assala Nasri performs in Damascus on September 17, 2026, after a 15-year absence. That removed a crucial route for <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/11/oil-prices-set-to-end-the-week-above-100-as-houthis-seize-key-port-city/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/11/oil-prices-set-to-end-the-week-above-100-as-houthis-seize-key-port-city/">exporting crude</a> and deepened fears of a global supply crunch.</p><p>On Thursday, Saudi Civil Defence officials said one person was killed and two were injured by debris from a Houthi drone that fell on Taif governorate, after it was intercepted and destroyed. A durable de-escalation remains the clearest scenario that could bring prices down.”</p><p>Gold, meanwhile, rose on Friday for a second straight day, to reach a one-week high. It came as investors continued to digest developments in the Middle East and oil kept moving down.</p><p>The precious metal, considered a safe-haven asset and a hedge against inflation, added 0.41 per cent to $4,378.48 an ounce.
