Abdulla Al Neyadi / UAE Presidential Court]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">Abdulla Al Neyadi / UAE Presidential Court</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[I’m eating here! In search of the perfect New York bagel, pizza slice, cookie and more]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/food/2026/09/18/new-york-food-tour-manhattan-pizza-bagel-bookie-peninsula-hotel/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/food/2026/09/18/new-york-food-tour-manhattan-pizza-bagel-bookie-peninsula-hotel/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Farah Andrews]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 12:04:00 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>“Where can I find the best bagel in New York?” It was a question I typed into Google several times in the run-up to my summer trip to the Big Apple, swapping out “bagel” for other New York staples, from cookies to slices of pizza.</p><p>I also asked around, quizzing friends who had visited and locals I met along the way. In a city as big as <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/new-york/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/new-york/">New York</a>, it was no surprise to receive an abundance of differing, often conflicting, opinions. So I took the decision out of my hands entirely, booking a food walking tour of Midtown Manhattan that promised some of the city’s most iconic bites, served with a generous side of history from an expert guide.</p><p>We were picked up from our hotel by Ben, who asked us a few questions to kick things off – “Is there anything in particular you want to try or see?”; “Are you happy to walk for three hours?”; and, most importantly, “Are you hungry?”.</p><p>My husband and I assured him we were indeed very ready to eat, and eagerly set off from <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/travel/2026/09/17/the-peninsula-new-york-review-a-quiet-corner-in-manhattans-midtown/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/travel/2026/09/17/the-peninsula-new-york-review-a-quiet-corner-in-manhattans-midtown/">The Peninsula New York</a> in search of some of the city’s best food.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/ESJ6MY5X3NEGZISCGSC5XBYV6M.jpg?auth=bf003622ed4df9d9771e09adb37e036017a2fb99022ba3180e8f10e517b455b7&smart=true&width=6136&height=4091" alt="Black Seed Bagels marks the first stop on a food tour through Midtown Manhattan; the everything bagel with cream cheese comes highly recommended. Photo: Black Seed Bagels" height="4091" width="6136"/><p>Five blocks south, down Fifth Avenue, our tour begins as any classic New York day should: with a bagel. Our first stop is Black Seed Bagels at Rockefeller Centre, where an everything bagel with a generous spread of cream cheese ($2.25) comes with our first taste of the city’s food history – and one of its most enduring debates: what exactly makes a New York bagel a New York bagel?</p><p>According to local lore, the answer is in the water. Flowing down from the Catskill Mountains, New York’s water has a mineral composition long credited with giving the city’s bagels – and its pizza – their distinctive texture and taste. Whether you buy the theory or not, it makes for a good story while eating your way through Manhattan.</p><p>Rockefeller Centre provided our first history lesson, as Ben pointed out Radio City Music Hall, the complex’s Art Deco architecture and the area’s diplomatic past. But the most intriguing detail was one easily missed on a hurried walk through Midtown: Cartier’s reproduction of the Question Mark, the first plane to fly non-stop from Paris to New York. The scale model, also known as Le Point d’Interrogation, is rendered in sterling silver and was a gift from the French government to Rockefeller Centre, presented in 1933.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/4AKDMHFWQZEKDG7SAT63VKGTSU.jpeg?auth=9f62ef3240f030d107ea293006bb96485afaa3235843a0455fadb2690fd2d568&smart=true&width=4368&height=2912" alt="Bryant Park offers a moment of calm – and a chance to walk off lunch – in the heart of Midtown. Photo: New York City Tourism + Conventions" height="2912" width="4368"/><p>From here, we walked through Times Square – an unavoidable rush of lights, screens and tourists – before arriving at John’s Pizzeria of Times Square on West 44th Street.</p><p>The restaurant is housed in a former church, and it still has plenty of the building’s character. It is also notable for its coal-fired brick ovens, which have been broadly phased out in New York, but a handful of pizzerias are still permitted to use under grandfathered licences.</p><p>Unlike many of the city’s famous pizzerias, there are no slices here: pizzas come as whole pies. Our 16-inch margherita ($29.50) arrived with a smoky crust from the coal-fired oven, topped with an almost sweet tomato sauce, mozzarella and basil. Spectacularly fresh, it was easily large enough for a couple of slices each for my party of three.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/QCGY66RV2RFJNPXJUAYS2W4RNQ.jpg?auth=e0b71c81e431aa7bda5c8a063af9ee1f0d2dccdfbe3d8e5f8b7465c9a7ad5224&smart=true&width=1500&height=2249" alt="Angelina Paris serves its rich chocolate hot or cold, with the latter offering some respite during a Manhattan summer. Photo: Mary Devinat" height="2249" width="1500"/><p>Next came a different kind of indulgence: hot or cold chocolate at Angelina Paris on Sixth Avenue. Given the summer heat, I opted for the cold version ($10), made with imported cocoa and served over ice. Rich, thick and almost dessert-like, it is not for the faint-hearted.</p><p>If drinking your chocolate isn’t your thing, detour to The Nook on Broadway for one of the city’s famous Levain Bakery cookies. Its chunky chocolate chip walnut ($5) is an essential New York treat, whether eaten on the go or stashed for later.</p><p>Our journey then took us through Bryant Park, one of Midtown’s most pleasant pockets of calm. In summer, it hosts free performances; in winter, a popular ice rink. Beneath it lies part of the New York Public Library’s vast underground storage system, home to millions of books.</p><p>From there, we followed Library Way along 41st Street, where literary quotations embedded in the pavement lead into Grand Central Terminal. Here, the trusty Ben really came into his own, sharing a wealth of historical insights – he has, he says, enough to fill a three-hour tour of the station alone.</p><p>Opened in 1913, Grand Central was modelled in part on European landmarks including the Paris Opera, and its scale is extraordinary. </p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/LRDISURWYJA2XBFOFMZ6KPBAXA.jpeg?auth=5f7494d1d7b2a8026108f443530a9c2ab364ac19751166a1602143041625adc0&smart=true&width=3024&height=4032" alt="Levain Bakery’s chunky chocolate chip walnut cookie has become one of New York’s most recognisable sweet treats. Farah Andrews / The National" height="4032" width="3024"/><p>The Main Concourse has a 33-metre ceiling, which was the largest indoor space in the US when it opened. That very ceiling is, perhaps, its most photographed feature, painted with constellations and signs of the zodiac.</p><p>Outside, Ben pointed out the famous clock, often attributed to Tiffany &amp; Co, although the claim has never been definitively confirmed, as well as the statue of Cornelius Vanderbilt. Inside, the food history continued at the Oyster Bar, the only original tenant from 1913 still operating today. With more time, it would have been high on our list.</p><p>Our final savoury stop was Joe’s on East 48th Street, where hearty steamed soup dumplings (from $13 for six) rounded out our roving feast.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/2UE3ZRDK3ZGQ3DEH4DO5XBFMPI.jpg?auth=9e97eb0133432a679859a7017bc61ae4916e51c6434ae8e287a2061192732a82&smart=true&width=6720&height=4480" alt="Steamed soup dumplings at Joe’s on East 48th Street provide the final savoury stop of the afternoon. Photo: Joe's Home of Soup Dumplings" height="4480" width="6720"/><p>From there, we wandered back up Fifth Avenue. With 8km under our belts, we were feeling suitably virtuous, so finished with a drink at Pen Top, the Peninsula hotel’s 23rd-floor rooftop bar. </p><p>Over sunset views of Fifth Avenue and the Midtown skyline, we reflected on an afternoon of very, very good food – and returned to the question that had started it all: did I find New York’s best bagel or slice of pizza? Who knows. But after an afternoon spent walking, eating and learning our way through Midtown, I’d happily recommend the search.</p><p>The private three-hour Iconic New York Bites tour is available through The Peninsula New York and costs $700 for up to four people.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/DDTEQY4OFJB53DCTLUC4PRO6EE.jpg?auth=119f0915b040a936400d1c7adb4774f53c6e8b41b0ae86f635181f84617d430a&amp;smart=true&amp;width=5616&amp;height=3744" type="image/jpeg" height="3744" width="5616"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[A coal-fired pie at John’s Pizzeria of Times Square, housed inside a former church on West 44th Street. Photo: John's Pizzeria of Times Square]]></media:description></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Extra patrols at London St Pancras station after crime gang arrested]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/09/18/extra-patrols-at-london-st-pancras-station-after-crime-gang-arrested/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/09/18/extra-patrols-at-london-st-pancras-station-after-crime-gang-arrested/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Tariq Tahir]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 11:20:49 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>Security at <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/london/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/london/">London</a>’s St Pancras station is to be stepped up after a spate of Algerian criminals targeting wealthy passengers and stealing their luggage.</p><p>British Transport Police (BTP) are to increase patrols of plainclothes officers around the <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/railways/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/railways/">railway</a> station, whose own security staff will also have a greater presence, after a number of publicised thefts.</p><p>BTP say intelligence shows <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/crime/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/crime/">thieves</a> will often lurk around Eurostar platforms – connecting Britain to mainland European cities – and try to trail people with expensive-looking designer bags before swooping at an adjacent taxi rank and carrying out the thefts while an unsuspecting victim’s eyes are turned.</p><p>Items of luggage are swiped from the pavement as the victim is about to put them into a boot of a car, or from an open boot as the victim is placing other items on the seats. In response, plainclothes officers will regularly be stationed at Eurostar platforms to identify potential offenders behaving suspiciously.</p><p>Their aim is to track them through the normally busy station and intercept then arrest them as they attempt to steal, usually at a nearby taxi rank.</p><p>There will also be announcements at the station every 10 minutes warning travellers that “bag thieves operate in this area”.</p><p>Private chauffeurs are being trained to be extra vigilant about luggage while picking up passengers.</p><p>The increased security comes after a wealthy family who arrived at the station on a Eurostar train and went to get a taxi were targeted by <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/algeria/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/algeria/">Algerians</a> Abdel Aitkebir, 35, and Medhi Fatih, 41.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/K2ZHKQOUFJDC3DUZIOZWDNDBOY.jpg?auth=649f849230c2bec77dd2bb97bd7786faaf983ec9a8d1d21b5aefc83b5812aa58&smart=true&width=4032&height=3024" alt="British Transport Police officers at St Pancras station in London. PA" height="3024" width="4032"/><p>As their luggage was being loaded into the car, the driver was distracted by an accomplice and Aitkebir grabbed two suitcases from the street and ran off. The cases contained items worth more than £600,000 ($800,000), including jewellery.</p><p>Shortly before the theft last October, Aitkebir and Fatih could be seen on CCTV scouting out the station. Photos of some of the stolen jewellery were later found on Fatih's phone.</p><p>Faysal Benoumechiara, 27, another member of their gang, was involved in a spate of thefts from trains or the back of cars in and around London railway stations.</p><p>These included a suitcase containing items worth an estimated £15,000 being snatched from a passenger at St Pancras after the thieves had again used distraction tactics.</p><p>BTP Superintendent Nick Sedgemore said specialist plainclothes officers are conducting regular patrols around the stations in response and are helped by CCTV cameras.</p><p>“We know how upsetting, inconvenient and costly luggage theft is and we are working tirelessly to crack down on this sort of offending at King’s Cross and St Pancras,” he said.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/5L3AGAYFCJHDTCPWW3TNTIHR7E.jpg?auth=04da53534e8a425dc3e1907c6b76643531fb1fb3571750992806c9d7c4d4d079&smart=true&width=5184&height=3456" alt="Wealthy passengers have been targeted by robbers at St Pancras. Getty Images" height="3456" width="5184"/><p>The London St Pancras Highspeed train company said it had asked its security contractors to patrol the station more often. </p><p>“The safety and security of our passengers is our highest priority, and these incidents are very concerning,” a representative said. “We have significantly increased security patrols around the station, working closely with both our own teams and the British Transport Police.”</p><p>Last week, another Algerian thief, Bilal Arbaoui, 32, was jailed for stealing a gold suitcase containing £175,000 worth of items outside St Pancras. In March, a police officer borrowed a bike to chase another Algerian, Hicham Labidi, who had snatched a suitcase from the boot of a taxi outside the same station.</p><p>The latest crime figures for UK stations show London Euston recorded 1,536 offences over the 12 month period. King's Cross recorded 1,479, while 1,074 were logged at London Bridge. St Pancras International recorded 1,035 crimes.</p><h2><b>Designer brands</b></h2><p>Other incidents at St Pancras involved thieves running off with a suitcase containing £250,000 of jewellery and designer handbags belonging to Brittany Button, the wife of the former Formula One driver Jenson Button. The couple had just returned from a city break in Paris. </p><p>At the same station, other incidents include the arrest of a man as he attempted to target a Brazilian family who were in the UK for a holiday. </p><p>Ramzi Rafadi pretended to retrieve items from a table as he took hold of one of their designer suitcases and discreetly wheeled it away. The bag and contents were worth about £5,000 but watching police officers swooped to arrest him. He was sentenced to 12 months in jail in June.</p><p>Fatih was sentenced to two years and four months in jail, while Aitkebir received two years and six months<b> </b>after each had pled guilty to one count of theft. Their accomplice has not been found.</p><p>Benoumechiara was sentenced to two years and three months in jail, having previously pled guilty to five counts of theft.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/47SVCXEFRZDM7MAPVSUWXMIKAA.jpg?auth=1048553a9e3094478fdcdeada31da3edba4a69b61d68ba8ba8c19cd82d4254bf&amp;smart=true&amp;width=679&amp;height=382" type="image/jpeg" height="382" width="679"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Three men involved in high-value luggage thefts at St Pancras and King’s Cross railway stations have been jailed, following a British Transport Police investigation. Photo: British Transport Police]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu"></media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Sheikh Mohammed bin Rashid explores project tracing cultural history of Al Khuzam]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/18/sheikh-mohammed-bin-rashid-explores-project-tracing-cultural-history-of-al-khuzam/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/18/sheikh-mohammed-bin-rashid-explores-project-tracing-cultural-history-of-al-khuzam/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[The National]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 11:19:06 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>Sheikh Mohammed bin Rashid, Vice President and <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/09/uae-should-have-best-government-in-the-world-says-sheikh-mohammed-bin-rashid/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/09/uae-should-have-best-government-in-the-world-says-sheikh-mohammed-bin-rashid/">Ruler of Dubai</a>, has explored a landmark research project documenting the history of Al Khuzam, which has deep roots in Emirati culture.</p><p>Sheikh Mohammed reviewed the initiative alongside Sheikh Hamdan bin Mohammed, Crown Prince of Dubai, Deputy Prime Minister and Minister of Defence; Sheikh Ahmed bin Mohammed, Second Deputy Ruler of Dubai; and Mohammed Al Gergawi, Minister of Cabinet Affairs and Chairman of the Executive Office of Sheikh Mohammed.</p><p>The research traces Al Khuzam's evolution through three distinct phases – its origins as a way of steering camels and navigating the desert; its transition into everyday social attire and lifestyle; and its modern role as a symbol of Emirati identity, state news agency Wam reported.</p><p>The project draws on historical evidence from across the Arab world and documents how Al Khuzam is mentioned in Arabic poetry, where it carried associations with camels, travel, leadership and resilience.</p><p>It will document traditional crafting methods, beginning with the collection of wool. Fibres are spun into threads and braided into rope, before knots, rings and silver chains are added to combine functional utility with aesthetic value. </p><blockquote class="twitter-tweet"><p lang="en" dir="ltr">Mohammed bin Rashid, in the presence of Hamdan bin Mohammed and Ahmed bin Mohammed, explores research project tracing the story of Al Khuzam from its earliest roots to its place in Emirati society today. The project looks beyond Al Khuzam as an object, uncovering how its meaning… <a href="https://t.co/IwCEATTQHW">pic.twitter.com/IwCEATTQHW</a></p>&mdash; Dubai Media Office (@DXBMediaOffice) <a href="https://x.com/DXBMediaOffice/status/2100842349995917341?ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">September 18, 2026</a></blockquote><p>The project frames the craft as an integrated system of knowledge, skill, materials and human stories. It also emphasises the need to pass on these skills to younger generations.</p><p>Beyond the preservation of the craft, the project explores how Al Khuzam's visual language – its distinctive rings, threads and details – inspires contemporary design. Emirati entrepreneurs and designers have already developed bespoke collections drawing on its symbolism, repositioning Al Khuzam as a cultural product with global relevance.</p><p>The project aims to empower artisans, designers and entrepreneurs to expand the presence of Al Khuzam in modern society while maintaining its connection with Emirati heritage.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/ONXB4B3ZJJGZRI7I722I3A2N4M.jpg?auth=2d0d9220e5a2c70ad9e674a2aa1b7f922f16f124973b67220cc131b6670b7ff1&amp;smart=true&amp;width=3950&amp;height=2962" type="image/jpeg" height="2962" width="3950"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Sheikh Mohammed bin Rashid views the research project documenting the history of Al Khuzam. Photo: Wam]]></media:description></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Warren Buffett steps down as Berkshire chairman after six decades]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/2026/09/18/warren-buffett-steps-down-as-berkshire-chairman-after-six-decades-as-son-takes-over/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/2026/09/18/warren-buffett-steps-down-as-berkshire-chairman-after-six-decades-as-son-takes-over/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Shweta Jain]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 11:08:35 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/2025/05/04/warren-buffetts-successor-greg-abel-expected-to-preserve-berkshires-culture/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/2025/05/04/warren-buffetts-successor-greg-abel-expected-to-preserve-berkshires-culture/">Warren Buffett</a> has stepped down as chairman of Berkshire Hathaway and been named chairman emeritus with immediate effect, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/2022/05/03/can-berkshire-hathaway-survive-after-warren-buffetts-departure/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/2022/05/03/can-berkshire-hathaway-survive-after-warren-buffetts-departure/">ending his more than six-decade tenure</a> in the company’s top leadership role.</p><p>Mr Buffett, 96, is to remain on Berkshire’s board as a director and will continue to provide his “judgment and perspective”, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/markets/2024/08/28/warren-buffetts-berkshire-hathaway-tops-1-trillion-in-market-value/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/markets/2024/08/28/warren-buffetts-berkshire-hathaway-tops-1-trillion-in-market-value/">the US company</a> said on Friday. </p><p>His son, Howard, a Berkshire director since 1993, has been elected chairman of the board.</p><p>“Serving as your chairman has been the privilege of a lifetime, and I have never taken your trust for granted,” Mr Buffett said in a letter to shareholders. "Father Time always wins. He has, however, been generous with me. He has given me the opportunity to see Berkshire reach a point where I am more confident than ever about what lies ahead."</p><p>Greg Abel, who succeeded Mr Buffett as chief executive this year, will continue to run Berkshire’s operations, Berkshire said in a statement.</p><p>Mr Buffett joined Berkshire in 1965 and transformed the company from a struggling textile manufacturer into one of the world’s largest conglomerates, with businesses spanning insurance, energy, rail, manufacturing, services and retail.</p><p>In the letter to shareholders, Mr Buffett said the time was right to complete the leadership transition and praised Mr Abel for exceeding his expectations since taking over as chief executive.</p><p>“My son, Howard, will succeed me as chairman,” <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/money/2024/03/20/why-warren-buffett-invests-like-a-girl-and-you-should-too/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/money/2024/03/20/why-warren-buffett-invests-like-a-girl-and-you-should-too/">Mr Buffett</a> said. “Greg runs the company; Howard will guard its culture and values – both worth more than anything on our balance sheet. Think of Howard as a policy the shareholders own and hope never to claim against.”</p><p>Howard Buffett has led the Howard G Buffett Foundation since 1999. Warren Buffett said he remained confident about Berkshire’s future and said the company was “in excellent hands”.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/7Y6OMBZWFRAD7CTY7HO5RQUY3Q.JPG?auth=4badaf2dbcedf389dea81878595a10e70ae43ace5f3e6564bc6fb89dbab24355&amp;smart=true&amp;width=2048&amp;height=1459" type="image/jpeg" height="1459" width="2048"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Warren Buffett says he remains confident about Berkshire’s future and that the company is 'in excellent hands'. Reuters]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">Reuters Photographer</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Omar Sharif, Shah Rukh Khan and Tom Cruise: A look back at Dubai International Film Festival's best years]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2026/09/18/diff-2027-dubai-film-festival/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2026/09/18/diff-2027-dubai-film-festival/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[David Tusing]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 11:05:50 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>For 14 years, December in Dubai meant movie premieres, packed red carpets and some of the biggest names in cinema descending on Madinat Jumeirah.</p><p>Now, after a decade-long absence, the <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2026/09/17/dubai-international-film-festival-to-return-in-december-2027-after-decade-long-break/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2026/09/17/dubai-international-film-festival-to-return-in-december-2027-after-decade-long-break/">Dubai International Film Festival is coming back</a>. The event, which ran annually from 2004 until 2017, will return on December 8, 2027, organisers announced this week.</p><p>During its original run, Diff grew into far more than a parade of international stars. It gave Arab and Emirati filmmakers a platform, helped finance productions that went on to international acclaim, and brought together Hollywood, Bollywood and the Arab film worlds in a way few other events in the region could.</p><p>There were plenty of unforgettable moments along the way, from Tom Cruise returning to the city where he <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2023/01/03/burj-khalifa-ring-emirates-video/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2023/01/03/burj-khalifa-ring-emirates-video/">scaled Burj Khalifa</a> and a young Ranveer Singh dancing his way along the red carpet, to landmark moments for Emirati, Saudi and Palestinian cinema.</p><p>As Dubai prepares to roll out the red carpet once again, here are some of the moments that defined the festival's first 14 years.</p><h2><b>2004: Curtain rises on Dubai's first film festival</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/24WCEMG4TZD2DBM73KL6T3JFDE.jpg?auth=c50bf74b92a68e22e56ec159535a977703067b34c14ede42d68482fde4605cc7&smart=true&width=2457&height=1970" alt="Orlando Bloom at the opening ceremony of the first Dubai International Film Festival in 2004. AFP" height="1970" width="2457"/><p>It all began on December 6, 2004, after almost two years of preparations.</p><p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/weekend/2023/12/15/timeframe-when-hollywood-a-listers-walked-the-red-carpet-in-dubai/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/weekend/2023/12/15/timeframe-when-hollywood-a-listers-walked-the-red-carpet-in-dubai/">For its first night</a>, Diff rolled out a 40-metre-long red carpet at Madinat Jumeirah, where 800 VIP guests gathered including Arab and international celebrities. Among them were <i>Lord of the Rings</i> star Orlando Bloom and Emirati filmmaker <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film/how-emirati-filmmaker-nayla-al-khaja-s-passion-project-led-her-to-cannes-1.730931" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film/how-emirati-filmmaker-nayla-al-khaja-s-passion-project-led-her-to-cannes-1.730931">Nayla Al Khaja</a>, who opened the proceedings.</p><p>The choice of opening film also signalled what Diff wanted to be. Rather than launching with a Hollywood blockbuster, organisers selected <i>Le Grand Voyage</i>, French-Moroccan director Ismael Ferroukhi's story of a father and son travelling from France to Makkah for Hajj. The festival's theme was Bridging Cultures, Meeting Minds, with Arab cinema intended to sit at the heart of an otherwise international programme.</p><p>Egyptian screen great <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/a-look-back-at-the-life-of-omar-sharif-1.46987" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/a-look-back-at-the-life-of-omar-sharif-1.46987">Omar Sharif</a> was the first actor to be honoured by the festival, with a retrospective featuring films including <i>Lawrence of Arabia</i>, <i>Doctor Zhivago</i> and <i>Monsieur Ibrahim</i>.</p><p>The first Diff screened 76 films and attracted more than 13,000 people. Thirty screenings sold out – a promising start for an event that would spend the next 13 years growing into one of the region's most prominent film festivals.</p><h2><b>2007: George Clooney and Sharon Stone bring Hollywood glamour</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/7KFWENNH2ZCZPDG3ABJR432IKI.jpg?auth=03a6776611bbf9caf24602773dc51bd0e265dcd99ab74cc367a0f59ac1928a04&smart=true&width=3000&height=2000" alt="George Clooney at the Dubai premiere of Michael Clayton. Getty Images" height="2000" width="3000"/><p>By its fourth edition, Diff was already attracting some serious Hollywood star power.</p><p>George Clooney arrived in 2007 with <i>Michael Clayton</i>, which opened the festival with its regional premiere. It was something of a return to familiar territory for the actor, who had previously filmed parts of <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/can-the-uae-cope-with-the-demand-for-movie-extras-1.123698" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/can-the-uae-cope-with-the-demand-for-movie-extras-1.123698"><i>Syriana</i> in Dubai</a>. Clooney also took part in an onstage conversation about his career during the festival.</p><p>Joining him on opening night was Sharon Stone, with photographs of the pair arriving together providing one of Diff's more glamorous early red-carpet images.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/TUXPNPUWG5HAXFGD43F6KG3HYY.jpg?auth=981cb8800ff01a5eb1cf851a11543adac4e75a55189aa270aabd9a80ec7088a1&smart=true&width=1706&height=3000" alt="Sharon Stone and Clooney at Diff in 2007. Getty Images" height="3000" width="1706"/><p>Stone had another role during the festival. As amfAR's fundraising chair, she presided over the inaugural Cinema Against Aids Dubai gala, which raised $3 million for HIV and Aids research and prevention projects.</p><p>The star-studded event also drew Michelle Yeoh, Gloria Estefan, Hayden Christensen and Dita Von Teese, offering an early indication of how ambitious Diff had become.</p><h2><b>2009: Omar Sharif meets Sheikh Mohammed</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/NKOUJ6MMKNFFPKRJCCAHLVGGFQ.jpg?auth=d5527a6a58a0770772d366bf7a1e426a3a4886a8ec9dfe15876424eca06f35b3&smart=true&width=3000&height=2083" alt="Sheikh Mohammed bin Rashid, Vice President and Ruler of Dubai, with actor Omar Sharif on the closing night of Diff in 2009. Getty Images" height="2083" width="3000"/><p>Even as international celebrities became regular fixtures on the Diff red carpet, the festival made celebrating Arab cinema central to its identity.</p><p>Few stars embodied the global reach of Arab cinema quite like <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/uae/sharif-brings-own-brand-of-humour-to-festival-1.547463" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/uae/sharif-brings-own-brand-of-humour-to-festival-1.547463">Omar Sharif</a>. The Egyptian screen legend, whose career stretched from classics of Egyptian cinema to Hollywood films, including <i>Lawrence of Arabia</i> and <i>Doctor Zhivago</i>, was one of the biggest names to attend the sixth Diff in 2009.</p><p>One of the enduring images from that year shows Sharif meeting <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/sheikh-mohammed-bin-rashid/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/sheikh-mohammed-bin-rashid/">Sheikh Mohammed bin Rashid</a>, Vice President and Ruler of Dubai, on the festival's closing night.</p><p>It was a fitting encounter for a festival that wanted to put Arab cinema and its stars on the same stage as the biggest names from around the world.</p><h2><b>2009: City of Life gives Emirati cinema its moment</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/CCS6EO3FKFDYZER7MKTZBWBBH4.jpg?auth=364b9ee2ca1d7206f8dd1a7e45b3c84fc2e5b1e58f93ee39d462646fb6a58e4b&smart=true&width=3000&height=2546" alt="Emirati director Ali F Mostafa, third from left, with the cast of City of Life in 2009. Getty Images" height="2546" width="3000"/><p>That same year also delivered a milestone much closer to home.</p><p>Emirati director <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/life-lessons-ali-f-mostafa-1.495439" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/life-lessons-ali-f-mostafa-1.495439">Ali F Mostafa's <i>City of Life</i></a> had its premiere at Diff, giving the UAE's emerging film industry one of its biggest moments yet. The ambitious multilingual drama, filmed in Dubai, follows the intersecting lives of people from different backgrounds living in the city.</p><p>At a festival accustomed to welcoming movies and stars from overseas, <i>City of Life</i> demonstrated that Dubai could produce its own stories as well.</p><p>The film would become an important marker in the development of Emirati cinema, while Diff continued to expand its support for UAE filmmakers through initiatives including the Muhr Emirati competition.</p><h2><b>2010: Colin Firth arrives with an Oscar-winner in waiting</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/YK64TXGHJZH7LAGOILTGQF5FPI.jpg?auth=4d5374960ff331504581825e88446928b732bd0fa118707eebf862e2921a9dcb&smart=true&width=3000&height=2193" alt="Colin Firth at the Dubai premiere of The King's Speech. Getty Images" height="2193" width="3000"/><p>Sometimes a festival screening becomes more significant in hindsight.</p><p><i>The King's Speech</i>, starring Colin Firth as Britain's King George VI, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/uae/colin-firth-proves-he-is-king-off-screen-as-well-as-on-1.558463" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/uae/colin-firth-proves-he-is-king-off-screen-as-well-as-on-1.558463">opened Diff in 2010</a>, with Firth among the stars attending the festival.</p><p>The film arrived in Dubai with considerable awards buzz, but had yet to complete the extraordinary run that would follow. A few months later, it won four Academy Awards, including best picture and best actor for Firth.</p><p>Diff audiences had seen one of the year's biggest films before it became an Oscar phenomenon.</p><p>It wasn't the first time. Two years earlier, the then relatively little-known <i>Slumdog Millionaire</i> had received a gala screening in Dubai before going on to dominate the awards season.</p><h2><b>2011: Tom Cruise brings Mission: Impossible back to Dubai</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/YHTORKLRRRCVHCNXK2SXKW67AM.jpg?auth=a0947b4a8db3182a77e94172a88ea6c7dc648fe064997a08351d21454ea6ced9&smart=true&width=3000&height=2167" alt="Dubai Ruler Sheikh Mohammed joins Tom Cruise at the Mission: Impossible - Ghost Protocol premiere. Getty Images" height="2167" width="3000"/><p>Few images better capture Diff at the height of its international star power than <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/tom-cruise-shah-rukh-khan-to-attend-dubai-international-film-festival-1.418440" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/tom-cruise-shah-rukh-khan-to-attend-dubai-international-film-festival-1.418440">Tom Cruise standing alongside Dubai Ruler Sheikh Mohammed</a> on the red carpet in 2011.</p><p>Cruise was in town for the world premiere of <i>Mission: Impossible – Ghost Protocol</i>, which opened the eighth festival.</p><p>The choice of Dubai was particularly fitting. The blockbuster had been partly filmed in the emirate, with Cruise famously climbing, running across and rappelling down the exterior of the Burj Khalifa for what became one of the franchise's most memorable sequences.</p><p>The premiere brought the production back to the city where one of its defining scenes had been filmed and demonstrated how far Diff had come since its 2004 launch. The emirate wasn't simply hosting a Hollywood premiere; it had become part of the movie itself.</p><h2><b>2011: Ranveer Singh dances his way along the red carpet</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/QSGGOMJQRVHX7MZRONZEUPKJIU.jpg?auth=4b880f33e9d9022773ab5381c9c289a17401887df458cdfab98571c040d7cbc8&smart=true&width=1996&height=3000" alt="Ranveer Singh at the Ladies vs Ricky Bahl premiere. Getty Images" height="3000" width="1996"/><p>Cruise wasn't the only star who made an impression that year.</p><p>A 26-year-old Ranveer Singh – now one of the biggest stars in India – arrived at Diff for the <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/new-indian-cinema-makes-a-showing-at-diff-1.357868" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/new-indian-cinema-makes-a-showing-at-diff-1.357868">world premiere of <i>Ladies vs Ricky Bahl</i></a>, his second film following his breakout debut in <i>Band Baaja Baaraat</i>.</p><p>Rather than simply walking the red carpet, Singh danced his way along it, entertaining the crowds and offering an early glimpse of the exuberance that would become part of his public persona.</p><p>“Every time there's an audience like this, I just feel compelled to break out in song and dance,” he told <i>The National</i> at the time.</p><p>Singh has gone on to become one of Bollywood's biggest stars, making his appearance a particularly fun snapshot of an actor at the beginning of his career.</p><h2><b>2012: Wadjda shows what Diff could do beyond the red carpet </b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/HLCHR6RK7VBODP4H5MCC2SBSDM.jpg?auth=40f98bcff515929ebfa0376140b2a09b4039704137daff7033f8443b8ffdc8c5&smart=true&width=3867&height=2310" alt="Saudi director Haifaa Al Mansour wins an award for her film Wadjda. Getty Images" height="2310" width="3867"/><p>Some of Diff's most important work happened away from the photographers.</p><p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/saudi-film-wadjda-tops-dubai-film-festival-1.367958" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/saudi-film-wadjda-tops-dubai-film-festival-1.367958">Haifaa Al Mansour's <i>Wadjda</i></a> became a landmark for Saudi cinema and female filmmakers in the region. The film, the first feature directed by a Saudi woman, received post-production support through Enjaaz, Diff's funding programme for Arab filmmakers.</p><p>It went on to win best film in the festival's Muhr Arab Feature competition and later became Saudi Arabia's first submission for the Academy Award for best foreign language film.</p><p>Its success demonstrated that Diff wasn't simply a place where Arab films were screened after they had been made. Through programmes such as Enjaaz, the festival was helping filmmakers get projects across the finish line and on to the international stage.</p><h2><b>2013: Omar puts Palestinian cinema centre stage </b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/BXRHEMVUCNGN7C2BJZZOE3MD7A.jpg?auth=9df5db6cd927b953328b3cff933e24f5aa6fe9acd6f50f49f31793bf39dfddb4&smart=true&width=3921&height=2670" alt="Palestinian director Hany Abu-Assad, second from left, with the Omar team. Getty Images" height="2670" width="3921"/><p>Diff celebrated its 10th anniversary with a statement of intent.</p><p>Despite having its pick of major international titles, the festival chose Palestinian director <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/hany-abu-assad-presents-a-direct-hit-from-the-west-bank-at-diff-1.651185" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/hany-abu-assad-presents-a-direct-hit-from-the-west-bank-at-diff-1.651185">Hany Abu-Assad's <i>Omar</i></a> as its opening film.</p><p>The tense drama, about a young Palestinian man caught between love, resistance and betrayal, had received post-production support from Diff's Enjaaz programme.</p><p>It went on to win the Muhr Arab Feature award at the festival and was later nominated for the Academy Award for best foreign language film.</p><p>The wider programme that year demonstrated just how central Arab cinema had become to Diff. More than 100 Arab films were included among 174 titles from around the world.</p><h2><b>2015: Zinzana fever hits Dubai</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/3NBY745MTFANLFAEATYA7JGM5A.jpg?auth=de8ec0c6f8f768ff6b23301c6d903d07a146b03396a083f1e7e1bbb75688636f&smart=true&width=3000&height=2000" alt="Emirati director Majid Al Ansari, third from right, with the cast of psychological thriller Zinzana. Getty Images" height="2000" width="3000"/><p>By 2015, Emirati films had become an increasingly central part of the programme.</p><p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/why-zinzana-star-ali-suliman-is-the-face-of-diff-2015-1.99226" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/why-zinzana-star-ali-suliman-is-the-face-of-diff-2015-1.99226">Majid Al Ansari's psychological thriller <i>Zinzana</i></a> arrived at the festival after earning attention internationally, including screenings at Fantastic Fest in the US and the BFI London Film Festival.</p><p>But bringing it home was different.</p><p>The film played to a packed audience at Madinat Arena, and Al Ansari summed up the significance of the occasion by saying everything that had come before was “all just a rehearsal for Dubai”.</p><p><i>The National</i> picked it as the movie moment of Diff that year – ahead of the splashier international films – while 12 Emirati films screened across the festival's 12th year.</p><h2><b>2015: Shah Rukh Khan causes red-carpet chaos</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/MFRTHBAXENDPFNGRDJZVPVY3NA.jpg?auth=0b64f92f2f31aaf76c5c38735f4803710bf2f94d1263ee80196c093f43946adb&smart=true&width=4494&height=2910" alt="Bollywood stars Shah Rukh Khan and Kajol with Diff chairman Abdulhamid Juma. Getty Images" height="2910" width="4494"/><p>There were Hollywood stars, Arab cinema legends and plenty of Bollywood royalty at Diff, but few could compete with the reaction Shah Rukh Khan elicited.</p><p>The Indian superstar's appearance at the Bollywood Gala on Fort Island in 2015 sent the crowd into a frenzy as fans attempted to get closer to him.</p><p><i>The National's</i> reporter recalled being “<a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/thats-a-wrap-highlights-from-the-12th-edition-of-the-dubai-international-film-festival-1.112337" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/thats-a-wrap-highlights-from-the-12th-edition-of-the-dubai-international-film-festival-1.112337">literally knocked off my feet</a>” by fans surging towards the actor.</p><p>It was a moment that captured the extraordinary relationship between Bollywood and its UAE audience.</p><h2><b>2017: Star Wars closes an era </b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/R6JG4NI2DRD3ZC5LOTXTZQ3D7A.jpg?auth=1efa31110d5a95f6b9db18f9eb4c523351b6c4c8117fd362112316fc46309e55&smart=true&width=5000&height=3314" alt="The festival came to a decade-long close with the screening of Star Wars: The Last Jedi. Getty Images" height="3314" width="5000"/><p>Nobody knew it at the time, but <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film/diff-2017-opens-with-celebrity-packed-red-carpet-1.682145" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film/diff-2017-opens-with-celebrity-packed-red-carpet-1.682145">Diff's 14th year</a> would be its last for a decade.</p><p>The festival bowed out in suitably blockbuster fashion, with <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film/diff-2017-film-festival-announces-140-film-line-up-1.677800" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film/diff-2017-film-festival-announces-140-film-line-up-1.677800"><i>Star Wars: The Last Jedi</i> closing the event</a> in December 2017.</p><p>Tickets for the gala were among the most sought-after of the festival, while <i>Star Wars</i> fans in costume joined the celebrations at Madinat Arena.</p><p>But the final ceremony also demonstrated how much Diff had grown beyond its international red carpets. It screened 140 films from 51 countries, while 13 films by Emirati directors were selected for world premieres.</p><p>Diff did not return the following year, and what initially appeared to be a pause eventually stretched into a decade-long absence.</p><p>When it returns in December 2027, it will do so in a very different regional film landscape from the one it left behind. But from Hollywood blockbusters and Bollywood frenzy to breakthroughs for Emirati filmmakers and films that helped reshape Arab cinema, it has plenty of history to build on.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/WWCKJQ5YF5HV3JC2ER5BTMS4IY.jpg?auth=cf60c95d5b91f1d74156e32dfe23966e506d8df05884ab5f24bd35bd97f9db64&amp;smart=true&amp;width=3000&amp;height=2027" type="image/jpeg" height="2027" width="3000"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Egyptian acting great Omar Sharif at Dubai International Film Festival in 2009. Getty Images]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">Andrew H. Walker</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Stokes believes Pietersen couldn't 'come close' to Harry Brook's talent … but KP will always have 2005 Ashes]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/2026/09/18/stokes-believes-pietersen-couldnt-come-close-to-harry-brooks-talent-but-kp-will-always-have-2005-ashes/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/2026/09/18/stokes-believes-pietersen-couldnt-come-close-to-harry-brooks-talent-but-kp-will-always-have-2005-ashes/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Ajit Vijaykumar]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 11:00:00 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>After Harry Brook smashed a sumptuous century against Sri Lanka in the first T20 this week, former captain Ben Stokes struggled to contain himself as he praised the white-ball captain and superstar in the making.</p><p>“I don’t even think the genius of KP could come close to what Harry Brook has talent-wise,” Stokes posted on X, adding, “You are an absolute freak, Harry. You’re mint to watch.”</p><p>That is high praise, coming from one of the greatest all-rounders of the modern era and from someone who knows the impact Kevin Pietersen, or KP, has had on English cricket.</p><p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/kevin-pietersen-and-sanjay-manjrekar-call-on-ipl-to-go-ahead-behind-closed-doors-to-kickstart-economy-1.1001648" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/kevin-pietersen-and-sanjay-manjrekar-call-on-ipl-to-go-ahead-behind-closed-doors-to-kickstart-economy-1.1001648">Pietersen</a> was the batter who started it all for England, at least this century. The bravado, the physique, the hair, the prickly temperament: they were all new and refreshing. Pietersen showed England that it was possible to intimidate the opposition with the bat – any format, any team.</p><p>The <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/2023/06/15/simon-jones-interview-2005-ashes-memories-injury-setbacks-and-why-ben-stokes-is-special/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/2023/06/15/simon-jones-interview-2005-ashes-memories-injury-setbacks-and-why-ben-stokes-is-special/">2005 Ashes series</a> was the defining moment of English cricket, and the first wall of the great Australian facade of the 21st century to fall. </p><p>While many would credit Andrew Flintoff and England's incredible pace attack for securing the famous 2-1 series win, it was Pietersen who actually ensured it with a superlative 150 in the final Test that saved the match.</p><p>Pietersen was the first truly great all-format England batter. He was the player of the series when England won the 2010 T20 World Cup – their first world title. And it was Pietersen who took it upon himself to create acceptance for the Indian Premier League in English cricket. Who can forget the toxicity directed at Pietersen for prioritising IPL and highlighting the benefits of franchise cricket for young England players?</p><p>It won’t be an exaggeration to say that Pietersen stumbled so others could walk. </p><p>After KP, England saw the meteoric rise of white-ball stars like Jos Buttler, Stokes, Eoin Morgan and many more. Not because of him, but certainly after him. The 2019 ODI World Cup triumph, followed by the 2022 T20 World Cup win, made England the preeminent force in world cricket.</p><p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/2026/01/29/harry-brook-as-unique-as-kevin-pietersen-and-might-need-special-care-from-england/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/2026/01/29/harry-brook-as-unique-as-kevin-pietersen-and-might-need-special-care-from-england/">Brook is possibly the next KP</a>. Or, according to Stokes, he is a significant upgrade on Pietersen. On paper, Brook has the stats and the game to add weight to that claim. The middle-order batter averages more than 50 after 41 Tests, compared to Pietersen’s average of 47 after 104. </p><p>Their numbers are largely similar in ODIs and T20s. However, numbers alone don’t make a player. </p><p>Stokes talked about the talent of <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/2026/03/06/harry-brook-backs-brendon-mccullum-to-carry-on-as-england-coach-despite-t20-world-cup-exit/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/2026/03/06/harry-brook-backs-brendon-mccullum-to-carry-on-as-england-coach-despite-t20-world-cup-exit/">Brook</a> being far greater than that of Pietersen. One can argue that Brook has a wider range of strokes and possibly looks better while batting. He is currently the only batter in the world to feature in the top 15 of the Test, ODI and T20 rankings. He is already a T20 World Cup winner, aged 27, with close to a decade of good cricket still ahead of him.</p><iframe width="560" height="315" src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/TcU-cRPP1fc?feature=oembed" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin" allowfullscreen title="Innings To Secure The Ashes! | Kevin Pietersen’s Superb 158 at The Oval | England v Australia 2005"></iframe><p>But Brook has not won a major tournament largely on his own as Pietersen did in the 2010 T20 World Cup or the 2005 Ashes. And KP will forever have the bragging rights of being the first player to challenge the might of Glenn McGrath, Shane Warne, Brett Lee and Jason Gillespie at their peak and wrestle the Ashes from their hands.</p><p>No other batter can ever lay claim to such a feat, simply because there is no ‘all-time great’ bowling attack anywhere any more. Sure, there are many potent bowling groups in world cricket, but no one has the experience, aura or numbers of the great Australian Test attack of 2005 that was fresh from a historic Test series win in India. </p><p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/mitchell-starc-believes-josh-hazlewood-s-accuracy-will-win-australia-the-ashes-1.675259" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/cricket/mitchell-starc-believes-josh-hazlewood-s-accuracy-will-win-australia-the-ashes-1.675259">Australia's quartet</a> of Mitchell Starc, Josh Hazlewood, Pat Cummins and Nathan Lyon are in a similar category, but they are not likely to be around together for long. </p><p>Brook could well end up with a better record than KP, have more world titles to his name and even win more Ashes series as a batter and captain, home and away. But Pietersen, currently guiding Brook as England's mentor, will always remain a trailblazer, a batter who took it upon himself to show how to bully the opposition, a player who made it cool to pursue personal game development through franchise cricket, a batter who helped secure a world title. And he will forever have the 2005 Ashes. </p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/GJWKWJ42KZQCU3D36ZBXLES55Q.jpg?auth=8bb2be65c78979cb65c336d02cb028a51e4b849355d9e5f60d9d7d581fccf7b2&amp;smart=true&amp;width=5134&amp;height=3271" type="image/jpeg" height="3271" width="5134"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[England batting consultant Kevin Pietersen, right, with Harry Brook during the first T20 against Sri Lanka in Southampton. Officials said 57 people were injured. </p><p>The ministry expressed ⁠its grief over ​the loss ​of ‌life and said the "cowardly actions" of ​the attackers could ‌not weaken the "nation's firm resolve". Mr Hameed described the incident as a suicide attack.</p><p>All ​seven attackers were killed in the ‌explosion and the gun battle ⁠that followed, police said in ​a statement. Videos shared by rescuers showed the boundary wall ripped apart by the blast, and people trying to pull the wounded from the debris as ambulances arrived.</p><p>The blast occurred close to a mosque where the police personnel, their family members and other civilians were offering Friday prayers, police said.</p><p>Local media has reported that an operation is under way to look for survivors, while the death toll may rise.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/DCXDOHUR3W4U43DPDZRBC5CBKU.jpg?auth=fb48fa803ba4bdd56352ade081b633f54a9b3ca40655653045598ed185fa3f20&smart=true&width=8160&height=4592" alt="People inspect the site of a blast at a mosque near the Old Police Lines in Kohat district of Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Pakistan. Tehrik-e-Taliban, the Pakistani branch of the Taliban and the region's main militant group, issued a statement disowning Friday's attack.</p><p>In recent months, militancy in Pakistan's border areas has surged, with the military and police the main targets. Islamabad says militants use safe havens in Afghanistan ​to train and plan attacks in Pakistan. Afghanistan's Taliban government has denied the allegation and said the militancy is a problem for Pakistan.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/I3VXQEUO2KDSO4H4PSRVETOS3U.jpg?auth=35ac80d2baec249f1137a204590312962104024219ac3bbeeb05614e7f5f49a8&amp;smart=true&amp;width=8160&amp;height=4592" type="image/jpeg" height="4592" width="8160"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Security officials inspect the site of a blast at a mosque near the Old Police Lines in Kohat district of Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Pakistan. EPA]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">BASIT GILANI</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[‘People can’t take it any more’: Syrian government cuts diesel prices after protests over fuel costs]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/18/syrian-government-cuts-diesel-prices-after-widespread-protests-over-fuel-costs/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/18/syrian-government-cuts-diesel-prices-after-widespread-protests-over-fuel-costs/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Nada Maucourant Atallah, Ahmad Fallaha]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 10:45:49 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/mena/syria/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/mena/syria/">Syria</a> has announced it will offer subsidised<i><b> </b></i>diesel for heating, agriculture and industry, days after <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/14/angry-syrians-hit-the-streets-in-protest-over-sharply-rising-fuel-prices/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/14/angry-syrians-hit-the-streets-in-protest-over-sharply-rising-fuel-prices/">demonstrations erupted</a> across the country over a fuel price rise<b>. </b></p><p>The decision has raised questions among experts about the effectiveness and durability of the measure, as broader social grievances continue to brew. </p><p>"People are frustrated and are waiting for realistic solutions to their problems in general," said one protester, Ayman Asfara from Idlib. "If they do not see results from the first protests, there will be further protests, but this time with greater awareness."</p><p>Energy Minister Mohammed Al Bashir said that the government recognised that higher energy prices “have a direct impact on people’s livelihoods and on the agricultural, production and service sectors”.</p><p>“Our responsibility is not limited to securing petroleum products. It also includes seeking solutions that ease the burden, while maintaining continuity of supply,” he added.</p><p>Benjamin Feve, a senior consultant at Karam Shaar Advisory, said the move could have harmful consequences for the economy and lead to a waste of Syria's already scarce public funds. </p><p>“Fuel subsidies distort prices across the economy, encourage overconsumption and create incentives for smuggling,” he said. “They are also regressive because the people who consume the most fuel benefit the most.” </p><p>Mr Al Bashir said subsidised diesel would be sold at 115 Syrian pounds ($0.94) per litre for heating, agriculture and consumers eligible for government support. A second grade would be sold at 150 pounds per litre for some production and service sectors.</p><p>The standard grade would remain at 175 pounds per litre, while petrol and gas prices would not change. The standard diesel price remains below its actual cost of 206 pounds per litre, Mr Al Bashir told a media conference in Damascus, where he was joined by Syrian Petroleum Company chief executive Yousef Qablawi.</p><p>Mr Al Bashir also said several small local refineries would resume operations under the management and supervision of the Syrian Petroleum Company, bringing their combined crude oil processing capacity to about 35,000 barrels per day. SPC chief, Mr Qablawi, said the company had begun drawing up an implementation plan for the measures after discussions with the ministry. </p><p>It remains unclear how long the subsidy will stay in place and how much it will cost the government. “Nobody can really answer where the money will come from except the government,” Mr Feve told <i>The National</i>. </p><p>Despite the lifting of western sanctions, Syria is still reeling from decades of economic isolation and years of war, which have left the country facing a reconstruction bill of hundreds of billions of dollars. “The budget is already deeply in deficit, by more than $1 billion. This was precisely one of the reasons subsidies were lifted in the first place,” he added. </p><p>The subsidies could be funded by allied countries, but this would risk making Syria dependent on foreign support and political agendas. “At the end of the day, it would be a donation, not a sustainable policy for the Syrian government,” he said.</p><h2><b>Widespread protests</b></h2><p>The decision to cut diesel prices came after a meeting with President Ahmad Al Shara on Wednesday night to discuss the fuel crisis that has led to protests, in a major test for the government that replaced the Assad regime.</p><p>The Ministry of Energy raised fuel prices by between 25 and 40 per cent last Saturday. The price rise came against the backdrop of the phasing out of what remained of the Assad government’s subsidy system, as the Syria's new authorities have pledged a shift towards a free-market economy.</p><p>The ministry said the increase in the cost of petroleum products reflected rising global oil prices and coincided with the temporary shutdown of the Baniyas refinery for a major overhaul, which has increased Syria’s dependence on more expensive fuel imports, Mr Al Bashir said. Global energy prices have increased sharply since the outbreak of the Iran war in February.</p><p>The move sparked some of the most widespread protests since the fall of the Assad regime, particularly in eastern and northern Syria, with demonstrators demanding that the increase be reversed. </p><p>Syrians rely heavily on diesel to run generators amid widespread power cuts. The fuel is also crucial for transport, adding further strain on a population already struggling with economic hardship after decades of western sanctions. About 90 per cent of Syrians live below the poverty line, UN estimates show.</p><p>Protesters have also questioned how Syria’s highly publicised economic reopening has seemingly benefited only a fortunate few, who have been displaying wealth rarely seen before, while doing little to ease the hardship faced by struggling households.</p><p>“Fuel prices went up, while at the same time people could see with their own eyes delegations coming and going, cars being replaced, new cars in the hands of a certain group of people,” Mr Asfara from Idlib, who took part in the protests, told <i>The National</i>.<i> </i>“At the same time, Syrians don’t have enough to eat, drink or to afford medical care.”</p><p>He said the fuel protests were just one symptom of a growing divide between social classes, meaning people were either very poor or very wealthy. There has also been a "complete absence" of support for the reconstruction and repair of homes, he said. </p><p>“People didn’t take to the streets just because of fuel. It was similar during the revolution: people weren’t protesting only because of what happened to the children of Deraa.” </p><p>Fuel was the trigger, Mr Feve said, but it may not have been the main cause. “Grievances have been accumulating, particularly in rural areas and outside the main cities.”</p><p>The difference, he added, is the breadth of the protests, which have spread beyond sectors. “Fuel touches everyone. It affects transport, generators, the price of bread, the cost of getting to work and heating your home.”</p><p>“It is one price that turns a series of separate complaints into a single shared grievance,” he said. </p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/QU3FRC6ZZEKKE5LY6XA6Z2TB7Q.jpg?auth=8fe104ab46e2492f4b5d6fd3d900b04ab2686cc2383c061f068c8ad68006591e&amp;smart=true&amp;width=6000&amp;height=4000" type="image/jpeg" height="4000" width="6000"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Oil tankers in Syria’s north-eastern Hasakah province. Syrians rely on diesel for transport, as well as to power generators. AFP]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">DELIL SOULEIMAN</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[E-scooter police patrols in Dubai recover stolen Dh190,000 in minutes]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/18/dubai-police-e-scooter-patrol-arrest-three-recover-dh190000/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/18/dubai-police-e-scooter-patrol-arrest-three-recover-dh190000/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[The National]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 10:44:48 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>Dubai Police have arrested three people suspected of stealing Dh190,000 ($51,735) – with e-scooter patrols recovering the money within minutes.</p><p>The command-and-control centre at the General Department of Operations were informed of the theft in the Naif area of the city and sent descriptions of the suspects to patrols in the area. </p><p>The force said <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/04/29/dubai-parents-must-do-more-to-stop-childrens-reckless-scooter-use-says-expert/">e-scooter officers</a> from the Naif Police Station reached the scene within two minutes and recovered the full amount only two minutes later.</p><p>Brig Omar Mousa Ashour, director of Naif Police Station, said the swift arrests and recovery of the money showed how effective patrols were in the city.</p><p>His station monitors patrols and manages how they are used in the area to ensure reports are handled quickly and police maintain a strong presence in busy areas.</p><h2><b>Riding to the rescue</b></h2><p>Brig Ashour said officers used e-scooters to move quickly through areas with a lot of pedestrians, and to avoid heavy traffic. The scooters also help police to move in places, like narrow alleyways, where conventional vehicles may be unable to go.</p><p>He added that Naif Police Station would continue to develop its field operations to further improve response times and enhance security efficiency.</p><p>In May, Dubai authorities launched a <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/04/27/special-unit-set-up-in-dubai-to-crack-down-on-unsafe-use-of-bicycles-and-e-scooters/">new patrol division</a> called the Personal Mobility Monitoring Unit. The unit was to ensure bicycle and e-scooter users follow traffic rules in the emirate.</p><p>The patrol was to "monitor unsafe behaviour and enforce violations related to the individual use of bicycles and e-scooters" and is a joint initiative between <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/dubai-police">Dubai Police</a> and the city's Roads and Transport Authority (RTA).</p><p>Members of the unit will monitor cyclists and e-scooter riders to ensure they wear helmets and protective gear and follow specified speed limits. The unit will crack down on reckless riding and also prohibit tandem riding on e-scooters.</p><p>The force has since expanded its use of e-scooters as part of its approach to policing busy areas.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/QJ37TLC4PRARRACWGWCV6TTA4I.jpg?auth=b904f572c22db35e6bb88e7e21a7d3e38fdbe50f4d816d47ccf1169a9d349611&amp;smart=true&amp;width=1414&amp;height=795" type="image/jpeg" height="795" width="1414"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[The swift arrests and recovery of the money showed how effective patrols were in the city. Police fired shots, injuring several people, <i>El Pais</i> reported. </p><p>The arrival of thousands of migrants over the summer caused a political headache for Spanish Prime Minister Pedro Sanchez, who has been accused of compromising the security of Europe by creating a backdoor to the continent.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/22LLD2VXQ6TUPLVVTTOGBMV2UQ.jpg?auth=231a0a844c3fd57417ae9ce3d85d39589d9777c5abcf7bb89839621667ad4f6e&smart=true&width=1620&height=1080" alt="Police prepare to move migrants to a new camp in Ceuta. EPA" height="1080" width="1620"/><p>Ceuta is one of only two European cities on the African continent – Melilla, 225km away, being the other – while Morocco is a key partner for Spain on economic, counterterrorism and migration issues. </p><p>Italy and ⁠Spain this week <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/europe/2026/08/17/ceuta-threatens-legal-action-over-migrant-crisis-pitting-italy-against-spain/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/europe/2026/08/17/ceuta-threatens-legal-action-over-migrant-crisis-pitting-italy-against-spain/">extended ​the suspension</a> of the European ⁠Union's border-free Schengen arrangement with each other for another 15 days. Italy said the decision ​was ⁠made because “significant risks to internal security ​persist, alongside a potential threat linked to possible terrorist infiltration”, the Interior Ministry said.</p><p>Mr Sanchez has insisted his government received no warning of the vast numbers who would arrive over the course of one weekend.</p><p>“No one could have anticipated the migration crisis that occurred on the 30 and 31 [of July]. No competent agency did. Not in Spain, nor in the EU,” Sanchez said in a post on X after declassifying a series of documents about the crisis.</p><p>Among the 40 documents and conversations released were alerts from July 29 about “a call to storm the [border] fence and come by sea”. They were circulated on Facebook and in WhatsApp groups with more than 180,000 members.</p><p>The communications from the intelligence services were passed to a low-ranking government official in Ceuta without anyone taking action. Those who were informed did not believe the threat would result in migrants arriving in such large numbers. And the warnings were not sent on to senior officials.</p><p>As a result, the release has triggered even greater scrutiny of Mr Sanchez’s actions and more questions about his accountability and competence. </p><p>For Spain's Prime Minister, the risk is that he miscalculated the degree of anger over the incident, including the ethics of going on a month-long holiday in the immediate aftermath and appearing to deflect blame onto Morocco. He also accused other EU nations of being “selfish” for not coming to Spain’s aid.</p><p>Opposition leader Alberto Nunez Feijoo of the right-wing People’s Party said the Spanish government was “incapable of grasping the true diagnosis and applying the correct treatment”.</p><p>“When a European border is violated, all of Europe is weakened,” he said. “When a border is not adequately defended, all of Europe is left unprotected. This is what is happening.”</p><p>He called for Europe as a whole to “acknowledge the failure of the Spanish government and exert the necessary pressure to prevent this crisis from becoming entrenched, spreading and recurring”.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/3AEBWCLE7RHCQOQOJWKHPJZORM.jpg?auth=ada96aebc94a246ac9f8c11a127daae66a5f0fd862e9072198bb880965813085&amp;smart=true&amp;width=1620&amp;height=1080" type="image/jpeg" height="1080" width="1620"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Migrants living at Benitez Beach in Ceuta wait to be transferred by Civil Guard members to a newly built centre. Photo: IIFA" height="900" width="1600"/><p>The IIFA Awards celebrate the best of Hindi cinema, bringing together actors, filmmakers, musicians and other figures from the Indian entertainment industry.</p><p>First held in London in 2000, the awards were created to celebrate Hindi cinema on an international stage and have traditionally been held in a different destination each year.</p><p>Previous host cities have included Dubai, Bangkok, New York, Amsterdam, Madrid, Kuala Lumpur and Macau. Dubai hosted the ceremony in 2006 before Abu Dhabi began its three-year run in 2022.</p><p>IIFA also has a sister event, IIFA Utsavam, which recognises achievements across the South Indian film industries, including Tamil, Telugu, Malayalam and Kannada cinema, while IIFA Rocks is dedicated to music and fashion. </p><p>The awards moved to Jaipur in 2025 for IIFA's 25th-anniversary celebrations. The location and dates for this year's IIFA Awards have yet to be confirmed.</p><h2><b>When and where are the IIFA Awards 2027?</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/X7TPYFTC5VGNRHR3Y4QM4BXMUI.jpeg?auth=2a2b67d473707cac23c218c91d3f045c5b79d6becffcc978b6637cdb81e3d561&smart=true&width=1596&height=752" alt="IIFA Rocks 2023 host Farah Khan. Photo: IIFA" height="752" width="1596"/><p>IIFA Weekend &amp; Awards will take place on January 22 and 23 at Etihad Arena on Yas Island.</p><p>It will be the fourth ceremony to be held in Abu Dhabi, following events in <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film/2022/06/04/iifa-awards-2022-full-list-of-winners-from-vicky-kaushal-to-kriti-sanon/" target="_blank" rel="">2022</a>, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2023/05/27/iifa-awards-2023-live-all-the-winners-and-highlights-from-abu-dhabi/" target="_blank" rel="">2023</a> and <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2024/09/28/iifa-awards-2024-live-winners-performances/" target="_blank" rel="">2024</a>, making the capital the event's longest-running recent home.</p><p>The 2027 IIFA Awards will once again be held in partnership with the Department of Culture and Tourism – Abu Dhabi and Miral.</p><p>“Returning to Yas Island for our fourth showcase is incredibly special,” said Andre Timmins, founder and director of IIFA Weekend &amp; Awards. “Abu Dhabi has embraced IIFA, our film fraternity and our audiences with extraordinary warmth, and together we have created some truly iconic moments. </p><p>"This IIFA, we want to raise that bar again, bigger stars, bigger experiences, deeper fan engagement and a celebration that reaches far beyond the arena.”</p><h2><b>Who is hosting IIFA Abu Dhabi 2027?</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/F4RB24R3SVBA7OL3BGGYBKLU6I.jpeg?auth=8229941d1a0554c62d56eeb9bdf4d93f726c14d6bbe16b9e8fc47c6201225f92&smart=true&width=1600&height=1066" alt="Varun Dhawan performs at IIFA 2023 in Abu Dhabi. Photo: IIFA" height="1066" width="1600"/><p>Director-producer Karan Johar and actor Varun Dhawan will host the main IIFA Awards ceremony.</p><p>They are no strangers to the job. Dhawan co-hosted the main awards in Abu Dhabi in 2024 alongside Shah Rukh Khan and Vicky Kaushal, while Johar has hosted several awards in India and abroad over the decades.</p><p>Pankaj Tripathi and Ali Fazal will host the IIFA Digital Awards, which celebrate work produced for streaming platforms. It will mark Tripathi's first time hosting an awards show.</p><p>“For an actor, there are certain moments that come with a little extra nervousness and a lot of excitement, and hosting the IIFA Digital Awards for the first time is definitely one of them,” he said.</p><p>“I have spent much of my journey standing in front of the camera and telling stories through characters, so stepping onto the IIFA stage in a different role is both exciting and humbling.”</p><h2><b>Who is performing at IIFA Abu Dhabi 2027?</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/LIB44M4LVFCB7DFUYGHMLGACNI.jpg?auth=740044f56025cb93b6b53edc19e410f536f44b87715e383962232a97e633f65c&smart=true&width=5663&height=3775" alt="Salman Khan promotes the IIFA Awards in Abu Dhabi in 2022. Khushnum Bhandari / The National
" height="3775" width="5663"/><p>Salman Khan, Shahid Kapoor, Kareena Kapoor Khan and Janhvi Kapoor have been confirmed as performers.</p><p>Salman Khan has a long history with IIFA and has performed at several editions of the awards.</p><p>“IIFA and I go back a long, long way,” he said. “I have been part of this journey since its early years, and every time IIFA calls, there is a special feeling because it is not just an awards show; it is a celebration of our films, our people and the audiences who have given us so much love.”</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/R765POEBMFDPBPRVQCDUB7DXDY.jpg?auth=3553e3057d581e5f3f2f296a222cbf7938174dbe146f898bb6b2878f234ec12a&smart=true&width=856&height=572" alt="Ananya Panday performs at IIFA 2024 in Abu Dhabi. Photo: IIFA" height="572" width="856"/><p>He said returning to Abu Dhabi held particular significance for him.</p><p>“I have performed at IIFA across the years, but coming back to Abu Dhabi always feels different. I have a deep connection with this city, and some very special memories here.</p><p>“Yas Island has become a beautiful home for IIFA, and the energy that the audiences bring is something you can feel the moment you step onto that stage. After everything we have created together over the years, I am looking forward to coming back, performing, having some fun and celebrating Indian cinema with everyone.”</p><h2><b>How much do IIFA Abu Dhabi 2027 tickets cost?</b></h2><p>Tickets are now on sale, with prices starting at Dh100.</p><p>The awards will be held at Etihad Arena, which has hosted all three previous Abu Dhabi ceremonies.</p><h2><b>How can I watch IIFA Awards 2027?</b></h2><p>For those unable to attend in person, there will be a new way to watch the ceremony in 2027.</p><p>IIFA has announced a partnership with YouTube that will see the awards livestreamed worldwide on the platform for the first time.</p><p>The partnership extends beyond the live ceremony, with celebrity conversations, behind-the-scenes access and creator-led content planned around the event.</p><p>Zee will also return as IIFA's official broadcast partner for a third consecutive year. </p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/JBEI5T2JCNFS5IWLD4OBHZYLEE.jpeg?auth=5169d8dc7d757cb96281d717640c49a92c946472c82e6955f4950ed80d80eda0&amp;smart=true&amp;width=998&amp;height=748" type="image/jpeg" height="748" width="998"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Shah Rukh Khan hosts the IIFA Awards 2024 in Abu Dhabi.
