Mullally]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 08:06:50 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>Tickets for Guggenheim Abu Dhabi go on sale today, at Dh70, ahead of the museum’s opening on December 11.</p><p>Visitors will be able to plan their first visit to the Frank Gehry-designed museum on Saadiyat Island, the largest in the Guggenheim network. </p><p>Its <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2026/07/28/guggenheim-abu-dhabi-opening-date/" target="_blank" rel="">opening date</a> was announced in July by the Department of Culture and Tourism Abu Dhabi.</p><p>The museum joins Louvre Abu Dhabi, Zayed National Museum, Natural History Museum Abu Dhabi and teamLab Phenomena Abu Dhabi in Saadiyat Cultural District. </p><p>Developed in partnership with the Solomon R Guggenheim Foundation, it will sit alongside the foundation’s museums in New York, Venice and Bilbao.</p><h2><b>How much are Guggenheim Abu Dhabi tickets?</b></h2><p>Standard adult admission is Dh70, while entry is free for under-18s. Children aged 12 and under must be accompanied by an adult at all times.</p><p>Senior citizens and UAE residents aged 60 and above can also enter free, as can people of determination, with a complimentary companion ticket available at checkout. Students at UAE universities will receive free admission, although proof of ID is required and tickets must be collected from the ticketing desk on the day of the visit.</p><p>Discounted admission is available for several other groups. UAE-employed educators, and members of the UAE military and Homat Al Watan pay Dh35, while Fazaa and Esaad cardholders pay Dh50. Proof of eligibility is required for the discounted categories, with tickets to be collected on the day of the visit.</p><h2><b>What will visitors see at Guggenheim Abu Dhabi?</b></h2><p>The museum has been developing its collection since 2009, with a focus on art from the 1960s to the present. Painting, sculpture, photography, installations, moving image and new media will feature across galleries exploring subjects including abstraction, popular culture, land, language and storytelling.</p><p>Previously announced artists include Jackson Pollock, Mark Rothko, Andy Warhol and Jean-Michel Basquiat. Discussing the <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/art-design/2025/11/21/first-details-of-guggenheim-abu-dhabis-collection-global-museum/" target="_blank" rel="">collection with <i>The National</i></a><i> </i>in November, Mohamed Khalifa Al Mubarak, chairman of DCT Abu Dhabi, said their work would appear alongside that of artists from the Arab world, Africa and Asia who have received less international attention.</p><p>Al Mubarak also described plans for a wider programme of activities: “It’s really a civic space. It’s a place that brings people together with music, food, dance and, of course, contemporary art.” </p><h2><b>How large is the museum?</b></h2><p>Gehry’s building covers a total built-up area of 80,000 square metres. It includes 11,600 square metres of indoor gallery space and 23,000 square metres of outdoor exhibition areas.</p><p>For comparison, the 1992 renovation and expansion of the Solomon R Guggenheim Museum in New York encompassed less than half that, at about 4,740 square metres of new and renovated gallery space.</p><p>Thirty galleries connect through a central atrium, while 10 sculptural cones rise across the site. Nine are clad in stainless steel mesh and one in onyx and glass. The cones reach heights of up to 88 metres and are designed to provide shade and natural ventilation.</p><p>Alongside its collection and exhibitions, Guggenheim Abu Dhabi will support commissions and research, with a multilingual programme giving visitors further ways to explore the art on display. </p><p><i>Tickets are available from the Guggenheim Abu Dhabi website</i></p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/LVGR2KMQIZEYLNIF3AGBM5RSF4.jpg?auth=991f462707b4caafda882752b3ceb2885691f096ff80279df87eecaf99f29d77&amp;smart=true&amp;width=8696&amp;height=5797" type="image/jpeg" height="5797" width="8696"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Guggenheim Abu Dhabi tickets go on sale from September 18. Lau]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 08:03:09 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>The theme parks at Dubai Parks and Resorts will reopen on October 7, after being closed for more than six months.</p><p>The destination – which brings together <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/things-to-do/2022/01/20/what-its-like-to-ride-motiongate-dubais-john-wick-roller-coaster-it-was-terrifying/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/things-to-do/2022/01/20/what-its-like-to-ride-motiongate-dubais-john-wick-roller-coaster-it-was-terrifying/">Motiongate Dubai</a>, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/things-to-do/2024/04/09/real-madrid-world-amusement-park-dubai/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/things-to-do/2024/04/09/real-madrid-world-amusement-park-dubai/">Real Madrid World</a>, Legoland Dubai Resort and <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/motoring/2024/02/23/supercars-parade-riverland-dubai/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/motoring/2024/02/23/supercars-parade-riverland-dubai/">The World of Riverland</a> – temporarily closed in March.</p><p>The reopening will also bring new experiences to Riverland, including a bowling attraction and Lamborghini Automobili racing simulators.</p><p>“The reopening of Dubai Parks and Resorts marks a significant moment for us and our guests. Bringing together world-class rides and immersive entertainment, family attractions, dining and staycations, the destination offers something for residents and visitors of all ages,” said Fernando Eiroa, chief executive of Dubai Holding Entertainment.</p><p>“As the season begins, we are building on this diverse offering with new experiences to discover, and more reasons to visit and return. We have an exciting season ahead, with much more still to come.”</p><h2><b>What's at Dubai Parks and Resorts?</b></h2><p>Dubai Parks and Resorts is home to three <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/things-to-do/2025/05/09/dubai-theme-parks-uae-disneyland-abu-dhabi/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/things-to-do/2025/05/09/dubai-theme-parks-uae-disneyland-abu-dhabi/">major theme parks</a>, including Legoland Dubai, which opened in 2016 as the first Legoland park in the Middle East.</p><p>It has six themed lands: Factory, Lego City, Imagination, Kingdoms, Adventure and Miniland, where 20 million Lego bricks are used to recreate landmarks from Dubai and across the Middle East. The theme park has more than 40 rides and attractions.</p><p>Legoland Dubai Resort also includes Legoland Water Park, which has 20 slides and water attractions, as well as <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/travel/2022/03/23/checking-in-at-legoland-hotel-dubai-where-every-child-is-a-vip-hotel-insider/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/travel/2022/03/23/checking-in-at-legoland-hotel-dubai-where-every-child-is-a-vip-hotel-insider/">Legoland Hotel</a>.</p><p>Motiongate Dubai also opened in 2016. The Hollywood-inspired theme park has 29 rides and attractions based on films and characters from DreamWorks Animation, Columbia Pictures and Lionsgate.</p><p>Themed areas include Columbia Pictures, DreamWorks and Studio Central, with attractions such as Madagascar Mad Pursuit, Dragon Gliders and The Hunger Games – Capitol Bullet Train.</p><p>The newest of the three theme parks is Real Madrid World, which opened in April 2024, replacing Bollywood Park. The football-themed attraction is spread across three zones: Champions Avenue, Celebration Plaza and Stars Avenue.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/ZBXJQEB2JJCDTHEQTT2KHQJKJU.jpg?auth=aa9d3c460eb0ab4e86241e6a7132c235beb52399cc4f30fa5760884257816190&smart=true&width=3840&height=2160" alt="Real Madrid World, which opened in April 2024, replaced Bollywood Park. Photo: Dubai Parks and Resorts" height="2160" width="3840"/><p>It has the region’s first wooden roller coaster and a ride called Stars Flyer, a 140-metre-high attraction billed as the world’s tallest swing chair ride. </p><p>Other attractions include the Bernabeu Experience and La Fabrica Training Pitch, while the park also has restaurants and shops.</p><p>Riverland, meanwhile, combines dining with games and attractions across four areas: French Village, Boardwalk, Sports Boulevard and The Peninsula.</p><p>Alongside the new bowling experience and Lamborghini simulators, activities include go-karting, archery and axe-throwing, mini golf, puzzle attractions and arcade entertainment.</p><p>The destination also has accommodation, including Legoland Hotel, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/travel/hotel-insider-lapita-dubai-parks-and-resorts-1.614311" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/travel/hotel-insider-lapita-dubai-parks-and-resorts-1.614311">Lapita, Dubai Parks and Resorts, Autograph Collection</a> and T-Rex Glamping.</p><p><i>Tickets to Motiongate Dubai and Real Madrid World start from Dh295, while entry to The World of Riverland starts from Dh25</i></p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/LFNJ5B6XTJF6RH4OPL2XUP6ZX4.jpg?auth=feb55e4052348c1f96f6cf7ae3179f71d6119ab32a633dc03200969cbd89a7f8&amp;smart=true&amp;width=5000&amp;height=2399" type="image/jpeg" height="2399" width="5000"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Motiongate Dubai is a Hollywood-inspired theme park. Dhabi]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/combat-sports/2026/09/18/ufc-333-countdown-one-night-two-title-fights-and-a-trilogy-to-be-decided-in-abu-dhabi/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/combat-sports/2026/09/18/ufc-333-countdown-one-night-two-title-fights-and-a-trilogy-to-be-decided-in-abu-dhabi/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Steve Luckings]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 07:59:10 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>UFC 333 is a little over a month away, but excitement for the big event is building.</p><p>One night, two title belts, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/combat-sports/2026/08/16/volkanovski-vs-evloev-and-yan-vs-dvalishvili-trilogy-bout-set-for-ufc-333-in-abu-dhabi/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/combat-sports/2026/08/16/volkanovski-vs-evloev-and-yan-vs-dvalishvili-trilogy-bout-set-for-ufc-333-in-abu-dhabi/">a three-fight trilogy to be decided</a> – there's plenty to look forward to when the world's lead MMA promotion returns to Abu Dhabi.</p><p>Alexander Volkanovski will look to add to his claim to be the greatest featherweight in <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/ufc">UFC</a> history.</p><p>The Australian (28-4) defends his title against the No 1-ranked Movsar Evloev on October 24 at Etihad Arena.</p><p>The unbeaten Russian extended his record to 20 straight victories with a majority decision over Lerone Murphy in March, which left many bewildered.</p><p>Evloev was deducted a point in the fourth round for repeated illegal groin strikes, yet somehow still did enough to convince the judges.</p><p>One of the best wrestlers in the game, Evloev initially changed tack against Murphy, deciding to stand and trade with the Englishman.</p><p>There is a case to be made that he came off second best in those exchanges. When Evloev reverted to type, he completely dominated Murphy. </p><p>Judges tend to lean towards a fighter who has control at the end of the round, but that deducted point, coupled with Murphy using Evloev's head as a human punching bag in the first two rounds, led most to score the fight a draw.</p><iframe width="560" height="315" src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/duyr3BGRa2Q?feature=oembed" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin" allowfullscreen title="Lerone Murphy Octagon Interview | UFC London"></iframe><p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/alexander-volkanovski">Volkanovski</a> was an interested observer, with the match essentially a title eliminator. The Australian, who arrives in Abu Dhabi off the back of two wins over Diego Lopes, warned Evloev he would be making a big mistake if he tried to stand toe-to-toe with one of the best to do it at 145lb.</p><p>"People are worried. People are going to sit there and go, 'Ah, is it going to be another boring fight?'" Volkanovski told Fox Sports Australia. </p><p>"He ain't holding me down. It's going to be a striking fight because I'm going to keep it there. He tried it in the Lerone Murphy fight to stand up, which I didn't like. I get it: He's trying to impress everyone and all that stuff.</p><p>"You're this undefeated guy with this style. I don't care if people think it's boring. Who cares? Let's see if that new style works with me – it ain't. Don't you dare try and just strike with me to entertain the fans. </p><p>“It's going to be striking because I'm going to make it. You come with your ultimate game plan, it's going to be exciting because I'm going to force you to fight on your feet, not because you're choosing to."</p><h2><b>Yan-Dvalishvili 3</b></h2><p>In the co-main event, Petr Yan will settle his trilogy with Merab Dvalishvili.</p><p>The Russian pulled off one of the outstanding performances of 2025 when he outclassed the Georgian over five rounds, avenging a loss two years earlier. </p><p>Yan puts his bantamweight belt on the line against the man he dethroned. Dvalishvili is bent on reclaiming what he believes is rightfully his. All the ingredients are there for a classic.</p><p>'The Machine' was one of the busiest fighters in 2025, defending his belt four times in a calendar year. </p><p>He fought Corey Sandhagen only two months before facing Yan. The 35-year-old acknowledged it was a mistake to accept the fight against Yan so soon afterwards.</p><iframe width="560" height="315" src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/HscShxH_JtI?feature=oembed" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin" allowfullscreen title="Петр Ян vs  Мераб Двалишвили 2 | Полный Бой"></iframe><p>“What was hard about last loss? I think I am better than Petr Yan,” Dvalishvili told the <i>Beyond the Win</i> podcast. </p><p>“When I took this fight two months before, I knew it was risky because two months is a very short time. When I fought Cory Sandhagen, I took the fight two months after, and it was a very short time.</p><p>“Against a tough guy like Petr Yan, you need rest and then a good training camp. But I didn't take rest and I had a hard training camp, which burned me out. No excuses, but I knew it was risky."</p><p>It was only a third defeat in 25 pro bouts for Dvalishvili. His loss to Yan ended a 14-fight win streak that included a comprehensive decision over Yan in their first meeting and saw him systematically dismantle and submit Sean O'Malley, weather a storm to come out on top against Umar Nurmagomedov, toy with Henry Cejudo, and dominate Jose Aldo.</p><p>The Georgian's stamina is the stuff of legend, but even he recognises that <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/mma">fighting</a> a rival with Yan's skillset requires rest and recovery. </p><p>After overexerting himself in 2025, he will make the trilogy fight against Yan his only fight in 2026.</p><p>Fully recuperated and with a full camp behind him, 'The Machine' will arrive in Abu Dhabi well-oiled and wanting to settle the debate once and for all.</p><h2><b>The undercard</b></h2><p>Flyweight prospect Lone’er Kavanagh will look to protect his top-six ranking and potentially move into the title conversation when he faces Ramazan Temirov.</p><p>A win over former champion Brandon Moreno put the rest of the division on notice, but a late submission at the hands of Brandon Royval has some questioning whether the likeable Londoner has the all-round game to challenge the likes of Alexandre Pantoja and belt-holder Joshua Van.</p><p>In Temirov, he faces a fearsome puncher, one who separated Steve Erceg from his consciousness the last time he was in <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/2026/07/27/ufc-abu-dhabi-the-winners-and-losers-from-fight-night-282-at-etihad-arena/">Abu Dhabi in July</a>.</p><p>It was his 13th first-round knockout. Temirov is confident no one can handle his power.</p><p>"We have a goal to take the belt, and it doesn't matter who will be next or in front of me. I haven't seen other fighters fighting like me – striking, punches, just making knockouts. I think the champion Joshua Van [has] got some striking, but my punches will just kill them all."</p><p>Alexander Volkov's victory over Jailton Almeida in Abu Dhabi 12 months ago put him next in line for a shot at the heavyweight title. By the end of UFC 321, those hopes lay in tatters.</p><h2><b>UFC 321 - in pictures</b></h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/X6UVTMBYUZFGJCJWJD5TZRQKC4.jpg?auth=5722b572a9e80782cd6b8a84192c0178878792d8b193dac70153415d4d77f25f&smart=true&width=6000&height=4000" alt="Ciryl Gane (black shorts) pokes Tom Aspinall in the eyes leading to a no contest at UFC 321 at Etihad Arena, Yas Island, Abu Dhabi. Tom Aspinall is still recovering from his injuries. Gane, meanwhile, is the interim champ, proving that there are levels to this game and that Alex Pereira’s ambitions only reach so far.</p><p>Volkov, a fighter more methodical than mesmerising, needs to put on a show against Rizvan Kuniev if he is to convince his UFC bosses he deserves a crack at Gane or Aspinall when he returns.</p><h2><b>One to watch</b></h2><p>One to watch outside the title fights is at light heavyweight.</p><p>Russian Azamat Murzakanov returns to action after suffering his first defeat in the pro ranks.</p><p>Murzakanov recorded his 16th win in as many bouts at UFC 321 with a first-round TKO victory over Aleksandar Rakic. </p><p>At that time, he looked the most fearsome puncher in the 205lb division. Six months later, he ran into the right foot of Paulo Costa, which altered both his equilibrium and trajectory. Expect him to display all his striking skills in his match against former title contender Dominick Reyes.</p><p>The American came as close as anyone to inflicting defeat on the legendary Jon Jones since Vitor Belfort almost snapped Jones' arm in half with an audacious arm bar attempt eight years earlier.</p><p>A three-fight winning streak was halted by current light heavyweight champion Carlos Ulberg. Reyes has since scored a split decision over Johnny Walker in a fight to forget.</p><p>Reyes has the tools, and questions remain over whether Murzakanov has the gumption to hang tough when things aren’t going his way.</p><h2><b>Other news</b></h2><p>The UFC announced on Thursday it will return to Qatar next month.</p><p>The event, to be held at ABHA Arena on November 21, marks the UFC's second time in Doha after debuting in 2025.</p><p>UFC president and CEO Dana White said: “When I visited Doha last year, I was blown away by the people, the hospitality, the food, and everything the city has to offer. </p><p>"Our first event there was a huge success – the fans were amazing, the arena was sold out, and the fights delivered. I’m looking forward to putting on another great night for the fans in Qatar.” </p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/CHIX3TOAKNEUTDCCZMBNLADDBM.jpg?auth=ee076aec58a591e6b4d2939a5482596c8e298ad8b5297eda1c685a50490fc3ef&amp;smart=true&amp;width=8145&amp;height=5430" type="image/jpeg" height="5430" width="8145"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Petr Yan, left, outclassed Merab Dvalishvili in their second fight to claim the Georgian's UFC bantamweight title in December 2025. Photo: Zuffa LLC]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">Chris Unger</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Control Resonant review: Remedy's weird world feels stranger than ever]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2026/09/18/control-resonant-review-first-impressions-release-date/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2026/09/18/control-resonant-review-first-impressions-release-date/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Faisal Al Zaabi]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 07:39:43 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>I had not realised how much I missed the world of <i>Control</i> until about five seconds into <i>Control Resonant</i>.</p><p>A return to the Oldest House, the imposing and impossible headquarters of the Federal Bureau of Control, immediately brought back memories of playing Remedy Entertainment’s 2019 original.<i> Control </i>was the sort of game that encouraged return visits, with its corridors packed with documents, recordings and strange details that made its world feel deeper with every playthrough.</p><p>Just a few seconds in, however, and <i>Resonant</i> made something crystal clear: this is a very different world.</p><p>The Oldest House is abandoned. The Hiss, the otherworldly force that served as the primary threat in <i>Control</i>, has escaped its confines and spread to New York City.</p><p>And Jesse Faden is no longer the character at the centre of it all.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/WK4UOHSDLVDGRCGBZO4KX3GCCE.png?auth=cf4b503d4c6a46fb24da3c3a95a3bc82d168f71a3652a19a2c76028c552e1abf&smart=true&width=3840&height=2160" alt="The sequel pushes Remedy’s paranormal world far beyond the Oldest House, with reality itself becoming increasingly unstable as the Hiss spreads through New York. Photo: Remedy Games" height="2160" width="3840"/><p>Instead, players take control of Dylan Faden, Jesse’s brother, who was introduced in the first game and now finds himself venturing into a city where the boundaries of reality have seemingly collapsed.</p><p>My first 40 minutes with <i>Control Resonant</i> at <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/pop-culture/2026/09/17/tokyo-game-show-2026-offers-glimpse-of-gamings-next-chapter-with-uae-in-the-mix/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/pop-culture/2026/09/17/tokyo-game-show-2026-offers-glimpse-of-gamings-next-chapter-with-uae-in-the-mix/">Tokyo Game Show</a> offered little explanation for how things reached this point. Dylan knows about as much as the player does, and that uncertainty works in the game’s favour. Players are stepping into a familiar universe, but one that no longer seems to follow the rules.</p><p>Visually, it is immediately impressive.</p><p><i>Remedy</i> demonstrated, along with <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/weekend/2023/07/14/alan-wake-remastered-review-replaying-the-bone-chilling-game-before-sequels-release/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/weekend/2023/07/14/alan-wake-remastered-review-replaying-the-bone-chilling-game-before-sequels-release/"><i>Alan Wake</i></a><i> 2,</i> how far its technology has come, particularly when it comes to lighting, animation and creating environments that could shift between the real and surreal. <i>Resonant</i> continues in that direction, with movement and animation feeling remarkably smooth even as the world around Dylan becomes increasingly chaotic.</p><p>The bigger change is in combat.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/L54LARC3VJG2TP4TCVBMFIRPWE.png?auth=560d2d69441965c5ca4dd55b6200c025238fc8039e1bc25e6a715777b6a85ad1&smart=true&width=3840&height=2160" alt="Combat in Control Resonant places greater emphasis on close-quarters encounters, while retaining the movement and supernatural abilities that defined the original game. Photo: Remedy Games" height="2160" width="3840"/><p>Whereas <i>Control </i>was built heavily around Jesse’s Service Weapon and supernatural abilities, <i>Resonant </i>pushes Dylan towards close-quarters encounters. Yet it did not take long to settle into its rhythm. </p><p>Returning players should quickly recognise the importance of movement, positioning and reaction to enemies, even if the tools are different, while newcomers are introduced to its systems without being overwhelmed by everything this universe has accumulated since 2019.</p><p>What has definitely survived is the weirdness.</p><p>If anything, Remedy appears determined to make things stranger.</p><p>The Hiss has warped New York to the point that the city itself is beginning to fracture. Streets fold over one another and buildings appear to defy normal geometry, creating images reminiscent of the collapsing cityscapes of films such as <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film/review-why-christopher-nolan-s-tenet-is-the-spiritual-cousin-to-inception-1.1066209" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film/review-why-christopher-nolan-s-tenet-is-the-spiritual-cousin-to-inception-1.1066209"><i>Inception</i></a> and <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film/2022/04/07/doctor-strange-in-the-multiverse-of-madness-release-date-cast-and-plot/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film/2022/04/07/doctor-strange-in-the-multiverse-of-madness-release-date-cast-and-plot/"><i>Doctor Strange</i></a>.</p><p>But <i>Resonant</i> finds beauty inside that chaos.</p><p>Colours, shapes and patterns emerge from the distorted environment until parts of New York begin to resemble <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/art-design/2026/09/16/christies-to-hold-first-abu-dhabi-fine-art-auction-ahead-of-landmark-uae-season/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/art-design/2026/09/16/christies-to-hold-first-abu-dhabi-fine-art-auction-ahead-of-landmark-uae-season/">abstract paintings</a>. There were moments during the demo when I wanted to stop moving simply to take in what Remedy had created, even as enemies were waiting nearby.</p><p>That combination of violence and visual experimentation is perhaps best demonstrated by the first boss encounter.</p><p>Known as The Artist, the creature is discovered inside an art gallery corrupted by the Hiss. The works around it have seemingly merged into a single nightmarish entity, transforming the gallery into both a battlefield and a piece of grotesque installation art.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/G3OLY4GFG5GT3D4F3OARHR4Z54.png?auth=e6b9a05587f85500158f3218bdd1673c0aab32daf5222708426ba745cb47ebc5&smart=true&width=3840&height=2160" alt="Remedy builds on the visual technology showcased in Alan Wake 2, using lighting, animation and surreal effects to push Control Resonant further into psychological and cosmic horror. Photo: Remedy Games" height="2160" width="3840"/><p>It is a suitably unsettling introduction to the creatures Dylan may encounter and one that feels distinctly Remedy. There are echoes of cosmic and Lovecraftian horror, but filtered through the studio’s fascination with architecture, art and the absurd.</p><p>More important, the encounter suggests that <i>Resonant</i> understands one of the things that made the original <i>Control</i> so memorable.</p><p>Its appeal was never simply about fighting enemies with supernatural powers. It was about wondering what was behind the next door, reading one more document or finding something inexplicable tucked away in a forgotten corner of the Oldest House.</p><p>Taking the action outside that building could have risked losing some of that atmosphere. Based on the opening, Remedy instead seems to have taken the strangeness contained within the Oldest House and unleashed it across an entire city.</p><p>After 40 minutes, <i>Resonant</i> had already done enough to whet my appetite. I want to see just how far Remedy can push this fractured version of New York when the full game arrives, at least until <i>Grand Theft Auto 6</i> consumes much of the gaming world’s attention.</p><p><i>Control Resonant is being released on September 24 for PlayStation 5, Xbox Series X/S and PC</i></p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/TBBTNMGRGZEK3B53NCGRVKR4LY.png?auth=4dbd2b788f6aa49a6bbd7806b124704555b36af2496a9b6db524e270baddf3ad&amp;smart=true&amp;width=3840&amp;height=2160" type="image/png" height="2160" width="3840"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Control Resonant puts Dylan Faden at the centre of the story, marking his first turn as the playable lead in Remedy Entertainment’s expanding connected universe. Photo: Remedy Games]]></media:description></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Hundreds join iPhone 18 frenzy as new models go on sale across UAE]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/18/iphone-18-apple-store-sales-dubai/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/18/iphone-18-apple-store-sales-dubai/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Nicole Abigael]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 07:38:35 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>Hundreds of eager <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/iphone/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/iphone/">iPhone</a> customers queued up at Dubai Mall on Friday morning to await the release of the latest models.</p><p>The iPhone 18 Pro and iPhone 18 Pro Max went on sale on Friday. National]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 06:26:31 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>The final round of the 2026 World Rally Championship has been moved from Saudi Arabia to Italy because of continuing unrest and uncertainty in the Middle East, with the season shortened to 13 rounds.</p><p>Rally Saudi Arabia had been scheduled to bring the championship to a conclusion in Jeddah from November 11-14, but the FIA has confirmed the WRC will instead finish with Rally Italia in Sardinia from October 1-4.</p><p>The Saudi event will still take place on its original dates, but will form part of the Middle East Rally Championship rather than the WRC calendar.</p><p>The FIA said Saudi Arabia remained ready to stage the rally safely, but cited “ongoing uncertainty in the region and its effect on logistical arrangements” for the decision.</p><p>FIA president <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/2025/12/12/mohammed-ben-sulayem-re-elected-as-president-of-world-motorsport/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/2025/12/12/mohammed-ben-sulayem-re-elected-as-president-of-world-motorsport/">Mohammed ben Sulayem</a> said organisers had explored every option to retain the rally as a WRC round.</p><p>“We have worked closely with our partners in Saudi Arabia, our Member Club the Saudi Automobile and Motorcycle Federation, WRC Promoter and our championship stakeholders in recent months to explore every possible route that would allow Rally Saudi Arabia to take place as planned," Ben Sulayem said. </p><p>“However, ongoing regional developments mean that the WRC element of Rally Saudi Arabia will not proceed this year. These are never easy decisions, particularly after the significant work undertaken by everyone involved in preparing for the event.</p><p>“While the WRC element of the event will not take place this year, our confidence in the organisers and in the long-term future of the rally remains unchanged ...</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/MJ3ABZXVHVH23OT6YJPMX2MDTI.jpg?auth=61fc1c50222fbdfb2e817d50731d255acebbac9ab94c79f33043443c72933e5c&smart=true&width=2511&height=1674" alt="FIA president Mohammed ben Sulayem, centre, says confidence in the organisers and in the long-term future of Rally Saudi Arabia remains unchanged. Photo: FIA" height="1674" width="2511"/><p>“Our focus now is firmly on the future. We will continue working together with our partners in Saudi Arabia and WRC Promoter towards bringing the FIA World Rally Championship back in 2027.</p><p>The decision adds another layer of uncertainty to a title race being led by Great Britain's Elfyn Evans.</p><p>Evans heads Finland's Sami Pajari by 17 points with a maximum of 35 available in Sardinia. The Welshman has finished runner-up in the championship five times and is seeking to become only the third British world champion, following Colin McRae and Richard Burns.</p><p>The move comes against a backdrop of conflicts across the region, including the Israel-Gaza war, the conflict involving Israel, the United States and Iran, and <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/17/houthis-assured-us-they-would-not-attack-american-ships-in-bab-al-mandeb-yemeni-sources-say/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/17/houthis-assured-us-they-would-not-attack-american-ships-in-bab-al-mandeb-yemeni-sources-say/">Saudi Arabia's continuing confrontation with Houthi rebels in Yemen</a>.</p><p>Motor sport has already been forced to alter its schedule this year. The FIA cancelled the Bahrain and Saudi Arabian Formula One races in April, with the <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/2026/07/26/malaysia-to-host-rescheduled-bahrain-grand-prix-in-october/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/2026/07/26/malaysia-to-host-rescheduled-bahrain-grand-prix-in-october/">Bahrain event subsequently relocated to Malaysia</a>. The Qatar and Abu Dhabi Grands Prix<a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/2026/08/23/f1-committed-to-finishing-season-in-abu-dhabi-calendar-is-confirmed/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/2026/08/23/f1-committed-to-finishing-season-in-abu-dhabi-calendar-is-confirmed/"> remain scheduled to go ahead</a> later in the season.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/MXST3PDZ7NETHDXDCOJOWYDVQ4.jpg?auth=ec637d85d0547553632d056ce69a9b98807f95fce08d6bc894666c2df3006864&amp;smart=true&amp;width=1822&amp;height=1214" type="image/jpeg" height="1214" width="1822"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Rally Saudi Arabia will proceed as scheduled from November 12-15 as part of the FIA Middle East Rally Championship. "For decades, Saudi-US security co-operation has contributed to regional stability, strengthened our collective defence capabilities and advanced our shared security interests," the Saudi embassy in Washington said on X.</p><p>Saudi Arabia has long sought to buy <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/us/2025/11/17/us-to-sell-f-35s-to-saudi-arabia-trump-says/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/us/2025/11/17/us-to-sell-f-35s-to-saudi-arabia-trump-says/">F-35 jets</a>, the most advanced US fighter aircraft. But <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/israel/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/israel/">Israel</a>, the only country in the region to possess the jets, opposed their sale to the kingdom despite a push for the two countries to establish relations. Israel has not yet responded to the State Department announcement. </p><p>News of the deal comes 10 months after Saudi Crown Prince Mohammed bin Salman visited Washington, where <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/donald-trump/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/donald-trump/">Mr Trump</a> initially said the US would allow the sale, while emphasising that Saudi Arabia is a major non-Nato ally.</p><p>Saudi Arabia is engaged in a conflict with the Iran-backed Houthi rebels in Yemen, with the group launching several attacks on the kingdom in recent weeks. They have also seized <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/11/houthis-seize-yemens-entire-western-coast-and-strategic-red-sea-islands/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/11/houthis-seize-yemens-entire-western-coast-and-strategic-red-sea-islands/">Yemen's entire western coast</a>, giving them unprecedented access to the Red Sea and Bab Al Mandeb strait. </p><p>The Houthis had reportedly been communicating with US officials to assure Washington that their latest advancements would not lead to attacks on American ships.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/V6ZNEYVV4VAKDLM53VIZEW55CU.jpg?auth=a19b5a052a15c96912466d27ad029e3cfc283190cecaadcf216c4b8ad2a95ff5&amp;smart=true&amp;width=1200&amp;height=800" type="image/jpeg" height="800" width="1200"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[ F-35 jets are the most advanced US fighter aircraft. The event was streamed live online on Syrian and Arab digital platforms and watched by millions. It marked a significant moment for those who supported the Syrian revolution.</p><p>The performance, titled The Return of Assala, had the backing of the country's Ministry of Culture and was one of the most high-profile cultural events since the <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/opinion/comment/2024/12/13/syria-assad-middle-east/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/opinion/comment/2024/12/13/syria-assad-middle-east/">fall of the Assad regime</a> in December 2024.</p><blockquote class="twitter-tweet"><p lang="en" dir="ltr">Assala, one of the Arab world&#39;s biggest stars, is not an artist I usually listen to. title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/11/houthis-seize-yemens-entire-western-coast-and-strategic-red-sea-islands/">Western powers</a> lack the military capacity or political analysts have said.</p><p>Whitehall officials have acknowledged that sending more UK forces to the region is “not politically expedient” and that Britain “doesn’t have much in the bank”, with military resources stretched.</p><p>A handful of UK and other western personnel are in Saudi Arabia discreetly discussing assistance with air defences, as Riyadh seeks to strengthen its ability to protect its AFP]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">-</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[What the Fed rate increase tells us about the economic environment ahead]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/markets/2026/09/18/what-the-fed-rate-increase-tells-us-about-the-economic-environment-ahead/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/markets/2026/09/18/what-the-fed-rate-increase-tells-us-about-the-economic-environment-ahead/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Tim Fox]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 05:00:00 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>With the US Federal Reserve having <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/economy/2026/09/16/fed-rate-rise-25-basis-points-hike/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/economy/2026/09/16/fed-rate-rise-25-basis-points-hike/">raised its benchmark interest rate</a> by 25 basis points on Wednesday to 3.75 per cent-4 per cent, its first increase since 2023, the immediate focus has now shifted to <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/economy/2026/09/16/has-the-us-federal-reserve-entered-a-new-major-tightening-cycle/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/economy/2026/09/16/has-the-us-federal-reserve-entered-a-new-major-tightening-cycle/">what its latest decision tells us</a> about the economic environment ahead. </p><p>More importantly, its updated projections point to another increase before the end of the year, while inflation is expected to remain above its 2 per cent target through 2027.</p><p>This is a significant change from the assumption that the next phase of the global interest-rate cycle would simply involve a gradual return towards lower borrowing costs. The more important development, however, remains the rise in long-term government bond yields.</p><p>The US 10-year Treasury yield recently moved above 5 per cent, while the 30-year yield has risen to levels last seen before the global financial crisis. </p><p>Long-term yields are not simply a reflection of where the Fed sets overnight interest rates. They incorporate expectations for inflation and growth, government borrowing requirements and the compensation investors demand for holding long-duration debt.</p><p>The message therefore goes beyond “the Fed may raise rates”. Investors are increasingly asking whether inflation will remain structurally higher, whether government deficits can continue to expand without pushing borrowing costs higher, and how much additional government debt the private sector is willing to absorb.</p><p>The Fed's latest projections reinforce these concerns. It sees inflation remaining elevated while economic growth and employment remain comparatively resilient. The Fed is therefore tightening policy because inflation remains too high despite a relatively strong economy, which makes the bond-market signal particularly relevant.</p><p>The same phenomenon is visible elsewhere. UK 30-year gilt yields are approaching 6 per cent, while European government bond yields remain substantially higher than during the post-financial crisis era. Japanese government bond yields have also moved sharply higher.</p><p>The possibility is therefore emerging of a global bond market in which governments increasingly have to compete for capital. That would represent a fundamental change from the post-financial crisis period, when central banks themselves became major buyers of government debt and exceptionally low, and sometimes negative, yields were treated as normal.</p><p>Normally, higher US interest rates and Treasury yields would support the dollar. The immediate reaction has indeed been positive, as short-term US yields rose and expectations of further tightening increased. But the longer-term relationship is less straightforward.</p><p>If higher US yields primarily reflect stronger economic growth and attractive investment returns, they should support the dollar. If they increasingly reflect concerns about fiscal sustainability, inflation and the supply of government debt, the relationship becomes more complicated.</p><p>The dollar can benefit from its safe-haven status while simultaneously facing questions about the longer-term risk premium attached to US assets. It may therefore remain supported in the short term, but sustained strength is not guaranteed simply because US interest rates are higher.</p><h2><b>Implications for the Gulf</b></h2><p>For the Gulf, the implications are particularly interesting. Most Gulf Co-operation Council currencies are pegged to the US dollar, meaning American monetary policy is transmitted directly into domestic financial conditions. Higher US rates therefore mean higher borrowing costs for Gulf banks, corporates, households and governments.</p><p>But the Gulf is not simply a victim of higher rates. The same geopolitical developments contributing to higher global inflation have also supported oil prices. Brent remains above $100 a barrel, providing a substantial revenue boost to the region's oil exporters.</p><p>This creates an unusual divergence. Gulf governments can face higher global financing costs at precisely the time that stronger hydrocarbon revenue are improving their fiscal positions. For countries with substantial sovereign wealth and relatively strong external balances, this is more manageable than it would be for highly indebted economies.</p><p>For sovereign wealth funds and large institutional investors, higher global bond yields are not necessarily bad news. After years in which attractive returns on high-quality fixed income were difficult to find, 5 per cent-plus yields on long-duration US government debt begin to look considerably more interesting.</p><p>The Fed's decision matters, but the more consequential signal may come from what happens to 10-year and 30-year Treasury yields in the months ahead. If long-term yields fall as inflation expectations become anchored, markets may conclude that the Fed has restored confidence in price stability.</p><p>If, however, yields remain elevated or rise further despite tighter monetary policy, the message will be different. It would suggest that investors believe the underlying problem is bigger than the policy rate: persistent inflation, large fiscal deficits, geopolitical risk and a growing supply of government debt. That would confirm the warning embedded in global bond markets over recent months.</p><p>The world may not be returning to the inflationary 1970s, nor does a return to ultra-low interest rates look inevitable. What markets increasingly appear to be pricing is something in between: a structurally higher cost of capital, greater volatility and a much smaller margin for policy error.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/UBALLNXRLOLTC4M37ZC4NIX2QY.jpg?auth=7393bcb259975adfe35c4ff7fc0b39605905a20fc639261042566c22996329cb&amp;smart=true&amp;width=5340&amp;height=3560" type="image/jpeg" height="3560" width="5340"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[The dollar can benefit from its safe-haven status but could face questions about the longer-term risk premium attached to US assets. the region, although Kraft did not specify which organisations would receive the money.</p><p>Kraft said Sheeran had contacted him before Macklemore issued his challenge and had asked him to commit $2 million. "Ed now plans to reach out to other venue owners and our goal is to continue to work together to build bridges," Kraft said.</p><p>The four have now announced pledges totalling $6 million, although only Macklemore and Ms Rachel have specified which groups will receive their donations.</p><h2><b>Why was Macklemore dropped from Ed Sheeran's tour?</b></h2><p>Macklemore was removed from the remaining dates of the Loop tour earlier this week after speaking in support of Palestine during two shows at MetLife Stadium in New Jersey.</p><p>During one appearance, he called for a "<a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/pop-culture/2025/02/21/public-opinion-pro-palestine-content-censorship/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/pop-culture/2025/02/21/public-opinion-pro-palestine-content-censorship/">free Palestine</a>" and told people in Gaza and the occupied West Bank that "we have not forgotten about them".</p><p>His comments prompted the Israeli-American Council to launch a petition calling for his removal.</p><p>Kraft subsequently confirmed that Macklemore would not be permitted to perform at Gillette Stadium, accusing him of a "broader history of antisemitic rhetoric and imagery". Macklemore has denied accusations of antisemitism.</p><p>Sheeran said on Tuesday that he had not personally removed Macklemore from the tour. He said several venue owners had objected to the rapper performing at their stadiums and that promoters ultimately made the decision.</p><blockquote class="instagram-media" data-instgrm-captioned isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/18/uae-and-sweden-arrest-international-money-laundering-gang-leader-in-joint-operation/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[The National]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 04:18:08 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>The UAE has arrested a Swedish fugitive wanted by Interpol on suspicion of leading an international money laundering network, in a joint operation with authorities in Sweden.</p><p>The suspect had fled Sweden and was the subject of an Interpol Red Notice when <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/08/25/uae-back-to-school-transport-safety/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/08/25/uae-back-to-school-transport-safety/">UAE security forces</a> tracked him down and detained him, state news agency Wam reported. The arrest was carried out as six other suspected gang members in <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/sweden/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/sweden/">Sweden</a> were detained, with legal proceedings initiated against all parties.</p><p>Investigations revealed the network had handled about 70 million Swedish krona ($7.1 million) over a 10-month period, processing cash proceeds from criminal activities and redirecting funds to other criminal entities. Authorities also found the gang used cryptocurrencies to transfer and conceal the value of those funds.</p><p>The tracing of cryptocurrency transactions and analysis of digital evidence helped uncover financial links to broader criminal activities, including organised crime and contract killings. </p><p>The operation was the result of direct security co-ordination between the UAE Ministry of Interior and Swedish police, with the countries also sharing evidence.</p><p>Brig Abdulaziz Al Ahmad, director general of the Federal Criminal Police at the Ministry of Interior, said the operation demonstrated the effectiveness of bilateral security co-operation. </p><p>"The UAE reaffirms its firm commitment to pursuing individuals wanted by justice and combating transnational organised crime," he said. "We will continue to track criminal networks, disrupt their sources of financing, and take legal action against anyone seeking to exploit the country's territory for criminal activities."</p><p>Anders Wiberg, police commissioner and head of the international division of the Swedish Police Authority, praised the partnership, saying co-operation with the UAE was "a key factor in achieving the successful outcome of this case". He expressed gratitude to UAE authorities for their "valuable co-operation and support" in bringing the investigation to a conclusion.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/VZ2PZ6J3N5B3ZF6HJAYI4EEUWU.JPG?auth=8a5b319c27dc888ce1b2d34bbd60a91db436c54230b933c562d730c79640c2f6&amp;smart=true&amp;width=4544&amp;height=2556" type="image/jpeg" height="2556" width="4544"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Interpol's headquarters in Lyon. Reuters]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">Denis Balibouse</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[AI is showing the corporate world why small is beautiful]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/opinion/comment/2026/09/18/ai-is-showing-the-corporate-world-why-small-is-beautiful/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/opinion/comment/2026/09/18/ai-is-showing-the-corporate-world-why-small-is-beautiful/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Yasar Jarrar]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 18 Sep 2026 04:00:00 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>For most of the <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/opinion/comment/2024/10/30/what-the-gulf-economies-can-learn-from-the-britains-industrial-revolution/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/opinion/comment/2024/10/30/what-the-gulf-economies-can-learn-from-the-britains-industrial-revolution/">past two centuries</a>, the economics of business rewarded scale. The bigger the factory, distribution network, workforce or balance sheet, the harder a company was to challenge. Size brought purchasing power, access to capital, armies of specialists and the ability to spread costs across millions of customers.</p><p>Modern corporations were built around a simple assumption: successful companies become big companies.</p><p>This is now changing, and fast, driven by the power of artificial intelligence, global connectivity and robotics; and it does not apply to firms only, it is a new paradigm in life, starting from national power.</p><p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/podcasts/my2020/2020/12/11/my-2020-podcast-thomas-friedman-on-how-mother-nature-has-the-whole-world-in-her-grip/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/podcasts/my2020/2020/12/11/my-2020-podcast-thomas-friedman-on-how-mother-nature-has-the-whole-world-in-her-grip/">Thomas Friedman</a> recently described in <i>The New York Times</i> what he calls the “new physics of power” reflecting on advances in warfare and how relatively small powers can use drones, software, precision weapons and asymmetric tactics to impose extraordinary costs on much larger powers. We also see smaller economies innovating and punching above their weight on the global economic scene.</p><p>There is also something similar, and arguably more exciting for us as individuals, happening in business. Technology has been lowering the cost of challenging incumbents for decades, but AI will be a powerful way of accelerating the process to the point where we need to rethink what corporate scale actually means.</p><p>There were clues long before ChatGPT entered our lives in 2022. When Facebook agreed to <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/comment/the-whatsapp-instagram-and-messenger-merger-what-s-really-in-it-for-facebook-1.819084" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/comment/the-whatsapp-instagram-and-messenger-merger-what-s-really-in-it-for-facebook-1.819084">buy Instagram</a> for $1 billion in 2012, Instagram employed just 13 people. In the same year, in the same business sector of photographs and memories, Kodak declared bankruptcy, with more than 150,000 employees. Two years later, Facebook agreed to acquire WhatsApp in a deal worth $19 billion, when the messaging service had only 55 employees. WhatsApp was already serving more than 450 million monthly users and adding more than one million registered users a day. Small companies could clearly achieve extraordinary reach, but there were hurdles to build and operate a global business. Now AI is beginning to remove that constraint, too.</p><p>Consider Lovable, the Swedish company that allows people to create software using natural lan
